# libspiffy code audit — 14 September 2026

| | |
|---|---|
| **Subject** | `lib/` of libspiffy 1.1.0 (50,600 lines), commit `a68d4a7` |
| **Occasion** | Upgrade to dactor 1.3.0, eventador 3.0.0, duraq 3.0.0 / duraq_isar 2.0.0 |
| **Dimensions** | Correctness, security, architecture, performance |
| **Method** | Five independent subsystem reviews (actors, event-sourced core, key management, storage, SPV and transaction building), every finding verified against the cited code path, then consolidated |
| **Outcome** | 99 findings. 27 fixed, 1 mitigated and 3 partially fixed in release 2.0.0; after backlog waves 1 to 3 and the follow-ups (97 table rows): 94 fixed, 1 mitigated, 2 partial, 0 open. Every fix carries a mutation-verified test ([section 2](#2-verification)); verification found and fixed 67 more defects ([section 11](#11-defects-found-while-verifying-the-fixes)) |
| **Companion documents** | `../dactor/doc/audit-2026-09-14.md`, `../eventador/doc/audit-2026-09-14.md`, `../duraq/docs/audit`; `CHANGELOG.md` entry 2.0.0 |

Line numbers refer to the pre-audit tree (`a68d4a7`). Status values:
**Fixed** (remediated in 2.0.0), **Mitigated** (failure is now loud, root
cause remains), **Partial** (part of the finding fixed, remainder tracked),
**Open** (tracked in beads; the bead id is given).

## 1. Summary

Counts are rows in the tables below and include fixes made after release 2.0.0 (backlog waves 1 to 3, marked "post-2.0.0"). Critical and High come from the findings index, Medium and Low from sections 7 and 8. The 2.0.0 release tally of 99 was counted by hand during consolidation and split a few combined findings (KM-3 / H1, KM-6 / H2, A-M5, A-L6) differently; the row count is 97.

| Severity | Fixed | Mitigated | Partial | Open | Total |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Critical | 7 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 7 |
| High | 25 | 1 | 0 | 0 | 26 |
| Medium | 39 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 39 |
| Low | 23 | 0 | 2 | 0 | 25 |
| **Total** | **94** | **1** | **2** | **0** | **97** |

No row is open. The partial rows are S-21 (`IsolateConfig` deprecated, not yet removed) and A-L6 (reply shapes still to unify, libspiffy-ro84). The purge parts of S-16 and M7 were rejected by design (section 7).

**By area**

| Area | Prefix | Critical | High | Medium | Low |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Actor layer | `A-` | 2 | 8 | 10 | 6 |
| Event-sourced core | `C1`, `H`, `M`, `L` | 1 | 5 | 10 | 5 |
| Key management and crypto | `KM-` | 0 | 2 | 3 | 4 |
| Storage backends | `S-` | 3 | 5 | 11 | 4 |
| SPV, network, transaction building | `SPV-` | 1 | 5 | 6 | 4 |

(KM-3 and H1, KM-6 and H2 describe the same defects from two angles and are
counted once, under the core.)

**The picture in three sentences.** The cryptographic primitives, the event
serialisation contracts and the SQL layer are sound. The defects cluster in
the seams: coordinators that bypass the aggregate, three storage backends
that disagree on semantics, an SPV layer whose trust boundaries are not
closed, and actor handlers that block their own mailbox. The one Critical
left open by 2.0.0 (S-01, the Postgres payment-channel read model) was
fixed in backlog wave 1.

No failure on the final tree is caused by the upgrade or by the fixes; the
list is in [section 10](#10-pre-existing-test-failures).

**Mutation verification of the fixes (added after release 2.0.0).** The
2.0.0 release shipped regression tests for only four of the 27 fixed
findings (A-C1, SPV-01, S-02, S-03), and none of those had been shown to
fail on the pre-fix code. Every finding marked Fixed, Mitigated or Partial
now has a `Test` row naming a test that was run twice: once with the fix
hunk reverted (it must fail with the reported symptom, quoted in the row)
and once with the fix restored (it must pass). A-H2's payment-coordinator
site is covered by a static guard rather than a behavioural test. Doing
this surfaced five further defects (ten more in backlog wave 3 and its follow-ups), listed in
[section 11](#11-defects-found-while-verifying-the-fixes); all five are
fixed and covered the same way. Epic libspiffy-hwz tracks the work.

## 3. Dependency upgrade notes

`dactor ^1.3.0`, `eventador ^3.0.0`, `duraq ^3.0.0`, `duraq_isar ^2.0.0`.
All four are on pub.dev; the eventador path override is gone.

| # | Change | Who is affected | What to do |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `IsarStorage` moved to `duraq_isar` | Hosts constructing DuraQ queues on the shared Isar | Import `package:duraq_isar/duraq_isar.dart` |
| 2 | `BitcoinWalletAggregate.preStart` is `Future<void>`; recovery runs inside it and dactor awaits it, so `spawn()` returns a recovered aggregate | Code calling `preStart()` directly; tests | `await` it. The 200 ms settle sleeps and the `RecoveryStatusQuery` poll were removed from `WalletManagerActor` and `InvoiceCoordinatorActor` |
| 3 | A host-supplied Isar must be opened with `LibSpiffySchemas.allSchemas` | Hosts sharing an Isar instance | `initialize` now throws an `ArgumentError` naming the missing collections. Under eventador 3.0 a projection whose checkpoint collection is missing stops in `ProjectionStatus.error` instead of replaying from 0, so the old partial-schema setup would leave read models silently frozen |
| 4 | `ask()` no longer retries | Nobody: libspiffy never depended on retries | The old default re-sent commands to slow aggregates up to four times |
| 5 | duraq 2.0/3.0 migrate the broadcast-retry queue database in place on first open | Deployments that might roll back | Back the file up; duraq 1.x cannot reopen it. `ARCActor` uses `processNext` (at-least-once), so the `dequeue()` change does not apply |
| 6 | eventador 3.0 tags `DateTime` in CBOR | Nobody | Every libspiffy `fromMap` already accepts `String` and `DateTime` |
| 7 | `PostgresEventStore` brought to the 3.0 `EventStore` contract | Postgres deployments | `Event.typeName` / `State.typeName` for type columns, `persistableMetadata` for the metadata column, replay-then-live subscription (see S-00) |

## 5. Critical findings

### SPV-01 · security · Fixed
**Unproven payment accepted without its inputs being covered by proven ancestors**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `lib/src/actors/spv_actor.dart:284-319, 410-460` |
| Failure | For a subject transaction with no BUMP, the actor validated every transaction in the BEEF that *had* a proof but never checked that the subject's inputs chained back to any of them, and `_validateTransactionSpendsCorrectly` skipped inputs whose funding transaction was absent. A BEEF holding one real mined transaction plus a self-signed payment spending non-existent outpoints validated, `_extractSpendableUTXOs` credited the outputs, and the invoice was marked paid. |
| Fix | Every input of an unproven transaction must resolve, recursively through the BEEF, to a transaction with a validated proof (BRC-62). An unproven transaction whose funding transaction is missing now fails script validation. Proven subjects are unaffected. |
| Test | `test/integration/full_spv_validation_test.dart` "rejects an unproven payment whose inputs are not covered by the BEEF". Mutation-verified: with the `spv_actor.dart` hunk reverted it fails (`Expected: false, Actual: true`); the other three tests in the file still pass, so it is the sole discriminator. |

### A-C1 · correctness · Fixed
**HeaderSyncActor polled its own mailbox for a header it could not receive while polling**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `lib/src/actors/header_sync_actor.dart:493-590`; `spv_actor.dart:202-208`; `integration/spiffynode_bridge.dart:133-140` |
| Failure | `_handleSpecificHeaderRequest` sent `getHeaders` then slept and polled storage inside `onMessage`. Headers arrive as `BlockHeadersReceivedMessage` on the same actor and are stored only by `_handleBlockHeadersReceived`, which could not run while the handler held the mailbox. Every opportunistic fetch timed out after 10 s, header sync was blocked for that period, and the asking `SPVActor` waited up to 15 s per validation. |
| Fix | Requests are parked in a per-height map and answered from `_handleBlockHeadersReceived`; a timer answers the timeout. `postStop` cancels the timers. |
| Test | `test/actors/header_sync_actor_test.dart` group "Specific header requests". Mutation-verified: with the hunk reverted both tests fail ("Timeout waiting for header at height 5 after 5s"; follow-up ask took 504 ms instead of returning immediately). |

### A-C2 · correctness · Fixed
**Client-side channel open stalled: `BuildFundingTransactionCommand` was sent to an actor that dropped it**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `lib/src/actors/channel_p2p_adapter.dart:178`; `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart:102-143` |
| Failure | On `channel_accept` the adapter told the channel manager a wallet command that only `BitcoinWalletAggregate` handles. The manager's `default:` discarded it, no `FundingTransactionBuiltResponse` was ever produced, and the refund/open sequence never started. |
| Fix | The adapter routes the command through the wallet manager with the coordinator as reply target (`ChannelP2PAdapter` gained `walletManager` and `updateReplyTo`, set from the coordinator's new `preStart`). |
| Test | `test/actors/channel_p2p_adapter_test.dart` "channel_accept routes BuildFundingTransactionCommand to the wallet manager". Mutation-verified: old routing gives `Expected: length 1, Actual: []` on the wallet-manager probe. |

### C1 · correctness · Fixed
**Deferred-spend UTXOs could never be marked spent; they returned to `available` after the reservation expired**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `lib/src/core/bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart:879`; `payment_coordinator_actor.dart:1099, 262`; `arc_actor.dart:534` |
| Failure | The coordinator reserves the inputs (2-minute expiry) and records the transaction with `deferSpend: true`, expecting ARC to send `SpendUTXOCommand` at SEEN_ON_NETWORK. `_handleSpendUTXO` rejected any UTXO not in `available`, so the spend never applied; the expiry cleanup then returned the on-chain-spent coin to `available` (balance overstated, coin re-selectable, double-spend attempt). |
| Fix | A spend now supersedes a reservation held by the same transaction (or with no holder recorded). |
| Test | `test/utxo_reservation_aggregate_test.dart` group "SpendUTXOCommand after reservation (audit C1)": spending with the reserving txid emits `UTXOSpentEvent`; a different txid is still rejected. Mutation-verified: `Bad state: UTXO test_tx_1:0 is not available for spending (status: UTXOStatus.reserved)`. |

### S-01 · correctness · Fixed (post-2.0.0)
**Postgres channel read model cannot work**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `lib/src/storage/postgres/postgres_wallet_storage.dart:1510, 1595, 1531-1541`; `projections/channel_projection.dart:142-166, 202, 224` |
| Failure | `ChannelProjection` passes Isar `PaymentChannelEntity` objects; Postgres casts them to `PaymentChannel` (`TypeError` on every channel event) and returns `PaymentChannel` where the projection casts to the entity. Even with the casts fixed, the `ON CONFLICT` clause never updates the server-side and funding fields, and `latestPaymentTxId`, `settlementTxId`, `errorMessage` have no columns. |
| Proposed fix | Type the interface on `PaymentChannel`, convert to the entity inside `IsarWalletStorage` only, extend the conflict clause, add the missing columns in a migration. |
| Fix | `ReadModelStorage` is typed on the domain `PaymentChannel`; only `IsarWalletStorage` converts to the entity. `ChannelProjection` persists `latestPaymentTxId`, `settlementTxId`, `errorMessage`. Postgres upserts every mutable column; migration v004 adds the three columns and drops NOT NULL on the funding/address columns of a requested channel. In-memory returns snapshots. |
| Test | `test/storage/channel_read_model_contract.dart` run against all three backends (groups "audit S-01"). Mutation-verified: old code `type 'PaymentChannelEntity' is not a subtype of type 'PaymentChannel'`; Postgres also `23502: null value in column "funding_tx_id"`. |

### S-02 · correctness · Fixed
**Postgres dropped `pluginMetadata`: token UTXOs were counted in the balance and selectable as payment inputs**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `postgres_wallet_storage.dart:601-713`; `migrations/v001_initial_schema.dart:129-149` |
| Failure | No column for `BitcoinUtxo.pluginMetadata`, so `getPaymentUTXOs` returned token outputs, `getBalance` summed them, `getUTXOsByPlugin` always returned `[]`, and coin selection could spend and destroy token outputs. Isar and in-memory both filtered. |
| Fix | Migration v003 adds `plugin_metadata JSONB` (indexed on `pluginId`); `upsertUTXO` persists it, `_rowToUtxo` hydrates it, and the three queries filter in SQL with the same `pluginId` rule as Isar. |
| Test | `test/storage/postgres/postgres_integration_test.dart` "keeps plugin-managed UTXOs out of payment UTXOs and balance". Mutation-verified: with the `postgres_wallet_storage.dart` hunks reverted: `Expected: {pluginId: pp1, ...}, Actual: null`. |

### S-03 · correctness · Fixed
**`block_headers.nonce INTEGER` could not hold uint32 nonces; header storage failed for roughly half of all blocks**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `migrations/v001_initial_schema.dart:283-296`; `postgres_wallet_storage.dart:963-1023` |
| Failure | `nonce`, `version`, `bits`, `timestamp` were int4. Any nonce ≥ 2³¹ (block 1's is 2573394689) raised "value out of range for type integer"; the per-row autocommit loop failed part-way, and `validateAndStoreHeader` reported the header as invalid. |
| Fix | v003 widens the four columns to `BIGINT`; bulk import runs in one transaction. |
| Test | "stores a header whose nonce exceeds int32". Mutation-verified: with v003 unregistered and the schema rolled back to v002: `22003: value "2573394689" is out of range for type integer`. |

## 6. High findings

### KM-1 · security · Mitigated
**`InMemorySecureStorage` is the silent default for every backend, including Postgres**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `lib/src/actors/libspiffy_actor_system.dart:275` |
| Failure | Events and read models are durable but key material lives in a Dart `Map`: after a restart every wallet exists and none can sign. The library ships no persistent `SecureStorage` other than the xpub-only Postgres one. |
| Done | A SEVERE log whenever a persistent backend falls back to in-memory secrets. |
| Still open | Make `secureStorage` required for persistent backends; ship a persistent implementation. |
| Test | `test/actors/libspiffy_actor_system_test.dart` group "secure storage default (KM-1)": SEVERE logged for Isar without `secureStorage`, not with one, not for `inMemory`. Mutation-verified: `Expected: length 1, Actual: []`. |

### KM-2 · correctness · Fixed
**BIP39 passphrase used to derive the wallet's addresses at creation but never stored or used for signing**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart:575` vs `:1279, :1848`; `payment_coordinator_actor.dart:798` |
| Failure | Addresses came from seed(mnemonic, passphrase), keys from seed(mnemonic, ''). Every payment received by a passphrase wallet was unspendable from inside it. |
| Fix | The passphrase is stored under `wallet_passphrase_<walletId>` with the mnemonic and used at every derivation site. |
| Test | `test/bitcoin_wallet_aggregate_test.dart` "signs with the BIP39 passphrase the wallet was created with" (proves the address depends on the passphrase, then signs; the aggregate's `correctlySpends` check validates the key). Mutation-verified: `SCRIPT_ERR_EQUALVERIFY: non-equal data`. |

### KM-3 / H1 · correctness · Fixed
**Two incompatible network-string conventions**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart:486, 695, 1048, 1231, 1819`; `wallet_projection.dart:184, 310`; `payment_coordinator_actor.dart:793`; `benford_coordinator_actor.dart:307`; `import_actor.dart:189` |
| Failure | The importer and actor system write `'main'` / `'test'`; the aggregate, projection and coordinators compared against `'mainnet'` in some places and `'main'` in others. Importing a mainnet WIF was rejected, a testnet key with `networkType: 'main'` passed, and on mainnet the output scanner decoded change outputs with the testnet prefix so change never became a UTXO. The projection also read wallet keys (`network_type`, `root_address`) that no backend returns. |
| Fix | `NetworkName` accepts every spelling; the aggregate persists the canonical one; the projection reads `network` / `rootAddress`. |
| Test | `test/utils/network_name_test.dart` (every spelling); `test/core/bitcoin_wallet_aggregate_mainnet_test.dart` (mainnet addresses, change output recorded, signing after scanning); `test/projections/wallet_projection_test.dart` group "network handling" (canonical network, preserved across metadata rewrite, mainnet prefix on received outputs); `test/integration/import_actor_network_test.dart` (mainnet WIF accepted by a `'main'` wallet, testnet WIF rejected; closes libspiffy-ecd). Mutation-verified: `Expected: a string starting with '1', Actual: 'miEUQ...'`; `WIF network type does not match wallet network type`; projection `Expected: 'mainnet', Actual: 'main'`. The "network lost on metadata update" symptom is only observable with the projection *and* in-memory hunks reverted together; the storage merge masks the projection key bug. |

### KM-6 / H2 · correctness · Fixed
**Multi-input signing verified input 0 against every UTXO**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart:1387-1394` |
| Failure | `inputIndex = 0` hard-coded inside the per-input loop. With SIGHASH_FORKID the signature commits to the input's own script and amount, so the check failed for any later input that differed; `correctlySpends` throws, so multi-input `SignTransactionCommand`s failed. |
| Fix | The loop index is used. |
| Test | `test/bitcoin_wallet_aggregate_test.dart` "signs a transaction with two inputs from different UTXOs". Mutation-verified: `SCRIPT_ERR_EQUALVERIFY: non-equal data`. |

### H3 · correctness · Fixed (post-2.0.0)
**Keys for change-chain addresses are never derivable**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart:703-709, 1267-1273, 1283-1289, 1837-1858, 2162, 2331`; `dartsv_crypto_service.dart:85, 202` |
| Failure | `generateChangeAddress` derives `m/1/{i}` but `derivePrivateKey` always derives `m/0/{i}` and ignores `isChange`; the aggregate records only the index (dropping `AddressDiscoveredEvent.isChange`). Funds on change addresses are unspendable through the wallet. |
| Proposed fix | Persist the chain with the index in state and in `SignTransactionCommand.derivationIndices`; make `derivePrivateKey` honour `isChange`. |
| Fix | `derivePrivateKey` honours `isChange` (m/1/i). The aggregate records each address's chain in state metadata `address_chains`, rebuilt from the journal (`AddressGeneratedEvent.purpose == 'change'`, `AddressDiscoveredEvent.isChange`; older events mean receive) and resolves it at every derivation site; optional `isChangeFlags` / `isChange` on the signing commands. |
| Test | `test/core/bitcoin_wallet_aggregate_change_chain_test.dart` (spend a change output the wallet created; replay round-trip; discovered change addresses). Mutation-verified: `SCRIPT_ERR_EQUALVERIFY: non-equal data`. |

### H4 · correctness · Fixed (post-2.0.0)
**Key material stored after the success reply; a failed secure-storage write leaves an unrepairable keyless wallet**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart:119-163, 169-200, 74-82` |
| Failure | `onCommandProcessed` replies success, then awaits `_storeKeyMaterial`. If that throws, the event is journaled, the caller was told success, and a retried `CreateWalletCommand` is silently dropped; every `GenerateAddressCommand` then fails. |
| Proposed fix | Write key material before `persistEvents` (compensate on persist failure) or at least before replying; answer duplicate `CreateWalletCommand`s deterministically. |
| Fix | Secrets are written to secure storage before the `WalletCreatedEvent` is persisted; a failed write fails the command with no event journaled; a failed persist removes the secrets (best effort). Duplicate `CreateWalletCommand` replies `success: false` instead of being dropped. |
| Test | `test/core/bitcoin_wallet_aggregate_key_material_test.dart` (mnemonic, WIF, xpriv; write-order log). Mutation-verified: old code journaled the event despite the storage failure. |

### H5 · correctness · Fixed
**WalletProjection double-counted an address's balance and usage on its first UTXO and never debited it on spend**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `wallet_projection.dart:329-358, 397-416` |
| Fix | New addresses are created empty and credited once by `updateAddressUsage`; spends debit the address. Replay idempotency of the delta-based balance remains open under M2. |
| Test | `test/projections/wallet_projection_test.dart` group "address balance (audit H5)" on a temp Isar: first UTXO credits once; spend debits to zero. Mutation-verified: `Expected: 50000, Actual: 100000`; after spend `Expected: 0, Actual: 100000`. |

**Timestamp BEEF was base64-encoded where ARCActor hex-decodes it**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `wallet_coordinator_actor.dart:890-901`; `arc_actor.dart:204, 250-256` |
| Failure | `hex.decode` threw, the failure was dropped (no sender), nothing was queued for retry, and `TimestampCompleteEvent(success: true)` had already been emitted. Timestamp archives were never broadcast. |
| Fix | The timestamp branch of `_handleBEEFPaymentResponse` sends `hex.encode(response.beefBytes)` (commit `8f7a276`). |
| Test | `test/actors/wallet_coordinator_beef_encoding_test.dart` "timestamp payment broadcasts the BEEF hex-encoded, as ARCActor expects" (captures the `BroadcastBEEFMessage`, hex-decodes and parses it). Mutation-verified: `FormatException: Invalid hexadecimal code unit U+0051`. |

### A-H2 · correctness · Fixed
**dactor's default 5 s ask timeout undercut every `AwaitEventApplied` window (10-30 s)**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | Seven sites in `invoice_coordinator_actor.dart`, `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart`, `payment_coordinator_actor.dart`, `wallet_coordinator_actor.dart` |
| Failure | Under a slow projection the ask timed out first: payments failed after the transaction was recorded (reservation dangling), invoice creation and channel open replied failure for operations that had succeeded. |
| Fix | Each ask passes an explicit timeout longer than the awaiter's window. |
| Test | `test/actors/invoice_coordinator_actor_test.dart` "a projection that replies after 6 s still yields success" (behavioural, invoice path); `test/actors/await_event_applied_timeout_guard_test.dart` (static guard over all seven sites: each ask timeout exceeds its awaiter window). Mutation-verified: `Ask request to actor slow-invoice-projection timed out after 5000ms`; the guard lists all seven sites when the timeouts are removed. |

| | |
|---|---|

| Failure | On mainnet every derived address has the wrong version byte, ownership checks fail, and received payments produce no spendable UTXOs. |

**Timeout-as-success UTXO reservation**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `payment_coordinator_actor.dart:1082-1128`; `benford_coordinator_actor.dart:517-556` |
| Failure | The aggregate replied only on failure, so every payment, provision and split waited the full 2 s, and a rejection that took longer than 2 s counted as success (double-spend risk). |
| Fix | `ReserveUTXOCommand` gets a `UTXOReservedResponse` on success and failure; no reply within 10 s is a failure. |

Mutation-verified: `No UTXOReservedResponse reply within 5 s`; with silence-as-success restored the payment proceeded on unreserved coins. |

### A-H6 · correctness · Fixed
**Failed address generation yielded an invoice with an empty address, reported as success**

| | |
|---|---|

### A-H7 · correctness, performance · Fixed (post-2.0.0)
**ImportActor cannot be cancelled and sleeps its way through imports**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `import_actor.dart:60-107, 113, 320, 337-354, 886-889, 974-977` |
| Failure | The entire import runs inside `onMessage`, so `CancelImportMessage` and `ImportProgressQuery` cannot be dequeued; 10 ms sleeps per address and 150 ms per transaction (1,000 transactions is 2.5 minutes of pure sleep); polls `getAddressCount` every 200 ms. |
| Fix | The import runs as a background job so the mailbox stays live; progress queries are answered and `CancelImportMessage` works mid-import. Every sleep and poll replaced by awaiting the real acknowledgement (projection awaiter or aggregate reply); an unacknowledged step fails the import. |
| Test | `test/integration/import_actor_cancellation_test.dart`. Mutation-verified: 30-transaction import 4.7 s -> ~80 ms; progress query timed out on old code; cancel produced `WalletImportCompletedEvent` on old code. |

### A-H8 · security, architecture · Fixed (post-2.0.0)
**Private keys are loaded and derived inside PaymentCoordinator and BenfordCoordinator**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `payment_coordinator_actor.dart:499-534, 782-833, 1430-1443`; `benford_coordinator_actor.dart:302-310, 449-503` |
| Failure | Bypasses the aggregate's `SignTransactionCommand` path and spreads key material across three components. KM-4 (plugin path signs every input with the first UTXO's key, libspiffy-y4b) and KM-5 (mnemonic wallets cannot sign in the coordinators, libspiffy-0im) are consequences. |
| Fix | Neither coordinator reads secure storage or derives a key. `AggregateSigningClient` sends signing requests to the wallet aggregate through the wallet manager with derivation index and chain from `AddressMetadata`. Standard and Benford paths use `SignTransactionCommand`; the plugin path builds in passes and signs each unsigned input through `SignMultisigTransactionCommand`, checking the signature recovers to the key hash in that input's locking script. Also fixes KM-4, KM-5 and the hard-coded `m/0` path (libspiffy-bu7). |
| Test | `test/integration/coordinator_aggregate_signing_test.dart` (7 cases, every input checked with `Interpreter.correctlySpends`). Mutation-verified: plugin payment from two addresses `SCRIPT_ERR_EVAL_FALSE` on input 1; change-address funding `SCRIPT_ERR_EQUALVERIFY`; mnemonic provision `No signing key available`; mnemonic Benford split produced no transaction. |

**Isar `getChainTip` returned the orphaned header after a reorg; `getBestHeight` ignored orphaning**

|---|---|

| Fix | Both walk the height index descending and take the first non-orphaned header. |
| Test | `test/storage/isar_wallet_storage_test.dart` group "chain tip after a reorganization (audit S-04)". Mutation-verified: `Expected: 2, Actual: 3` (best height counted the orphan); tip returned the orphan's hash. |

### S-05 · correctness · Fixed (post-2.0.0)
**Transactions and UTXOs are keyed by global txid**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `v001_initial_schema.dart:169, 133`; `libspiffy_schemas.dart:423, 275`; `postgres_wallet_storage.dart:854-861`; `isar_wallet_storage.dart:707-735` |
| Failure | Wallet A paying wallet B in the same store: B's `storeTransaction` is a no-op (Postgres) or throws on the unique index (Isar). `getTransaction(txid)` has no `walletId`. |
| Proposed fix | Uniqueness on `(wallet_id, txid)` / `(wallet_id, txid, vout)`; `getTransaction` takes a wallet id. |
| Fix | Transactions unique on `(wallet_id, txid)` and UTXOs on `(wallet_id, txid, vout)` in all three backends (Postgres migration v005; Isar composite unique indexes; in-memory keyed per wallet). `getTransaction(txid, {walletId})`; the projection and the coordinator pass the wallet id. Postgres `storeTransaction` updates amounts, direction and addresses on conflict. v005 down keeps only the first-stored row per txid / outpoint. |
| Test | `test/storage/read_model_keying_contract.dart` on in-memory, Isar and Postgres. Mutation-verified: Isar `Unique index violated` for wallet B's UTXO; Postgres and Isar missing wallet B's transaction row; in-memory overwrote wallet A's amount (`Expected: -1200, Actual: 1000`). |

### S-07 · correctness · Fixed (post-2.0.0)
**Invoice storage methods return three different types**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `libspiffy_schemas.dart:958-980`; `postgres_wallet_storage.dart:1399, 1349-1380`; `in_memory_wallet_storage.dart:750-775`; `invoice_coordinator_actor.dart:734-737` |
| Failure | Isar returns a `Map`, Postgres an `InvoiceReadModel`, in-memory the stored object; the coordinator accepts `Invoice` or `Map`, so Postgres and in-memory throw on `CheckInvoice` / `ListInvoices`. In-memory `updateInvoiceStatus` assigns to final fields. Postgres has no `outputs_json`. |
| Fix | Invoice methods return `InvoiceReadModel` in every backend; new `listInvoices({walletId, status})`; Isar `storeInvoice` upserts; Postgres stores `outputs_json` (v004); in-memory `updateInvoiceStatus` uses `copyWith`; the coordinator consumes `InvoiceReadModel` only. |
| Test | `test/storage/invoice_read_model_contract.dart` against all three backends; `test/actors/invoice_coordinator_actor_test.dart` group "S-07". Mutation-verified: old code returned a `Map` (Isar), dropped outputs (Postgres), `NoSuchMethodError: setter 'status='` (in-memory), `InvoiceReadModel is not a subtype of Map` (coordinator). |

### S-08 · performance · Fixed (post-2.0.0) · libspiffy-tyd
**Postgres bulk header import was one autocommit INSERT per header**

| | |
|---|---|
| Done | One transaction per batch (no partial chains). Post-2.0.0 (wave 3): headers are deduplicated by hash and written as multi-row upserts in chunks of 1,000 inside that transaction; S-12 upsert semantics (orphan re-activation) unchanged. |
| Test | `postgres_integration_test.dart` "stores a bulk batch atomically" (third header invalid; none of the three stored). Mutation-verified: `a failed batch must not leave its first header stored`. Wave 3: "S-08: a bulk batch is written as multi-row statements in chunks, with upsert semantics" (statement hook in the old per-row loop: `Expected: [3, 3, 1] Actual: [1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1, 1]`). |

### SPV-02 · security · Fixed (post-2.0.0)
**Header chain has no genesis/checkpoint anchor, no difficulty validation and no fork choice**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `spv/block_header_chain.dart:284-348`; `integration/spiffynode_bridge.dart:258-259` |
| Failure | The first header from whichever peer answers is accepted; PoW is checked against the header's own `bits`; the tip advances on `height >= bestHeight` with no chainwork comparison. A malicious first peer installs a fake chain against which SPV-01-style BEEFs "validate". |
| Fix | `BlockHeaderChain` takes `NetworkParams` and a `BlockHeaderAnchor` (default: genesis at 0), seeds it into an empty store and refuses an unanchored store. Heights are derived from the parent. Every header: target in (0, powLimit], hash at or below target, timestamp above the median of the previous 11 and at most 2 h ahead. Mainnet difficulty: exact legacy retarget, 4x bound through the EDA months, exact cw-144 DAA from 504032 (`difficulty_rules.dart`, verified against real headers 504027-504036 and 700000-700199). Testnet/regtest: pow limit and target only. Fork choice by cumulative chainwork with in-memory side branches. Limit: an old database body is not re-validated on startup, only its anchoring. |
| Test | `test/spv/block_header_chain_test.dart` groups "SPV-02 anchor / proof of work and difficulty / fork choice by chainwork", `test/spv/difficulty_rules_test.dart` (real mainnet fixtures under `test/data/`, re-hashed on load). Mutation-verified: non-genesis first header accepted, bits easier than the pow limit accepted, sign-bit target accepted, future timestamp accepted, lower-work branch became the tip, DAA-era headers `Expected: 700000, Actual: 0`. |

### SPV-03 · correctness · Fixed (post-2.0.0)
**Reorg handling is a no-op and reorg batches are mis-heighted; the wallet stalls on a stale branch**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `header_sync_actor.dart:397-416`; `spiffynode_bridge.dart:258-259`; `block_header_chain.dart:198-231` |
| Failure | `handleReorganization([], [])` changes nothing; every batch is assigned `startHeight = bestHeight + 1`, so post-reorg headers get the wrong height and are dropped forever. |
| Fix | `HeaderSyncActor` stores each header via `acceptHeader` (height from parent; `startHeight` ignored), counts real reorgs, notifies the SPV actor with `isReorg`, and re-requests with a full block locator when a batch does not connect. The peer handler derives `startHeight` from the first header's parent. `handleReorganization` runs through `acceptHeader` and moves the tip only on more work. Known limit: reorg back onto a previously orphaned branch is not persisted (bead filed). |
| Test | `test/spv/block_header_chain_test.dart` group "SPV-03 reorganization", `test/spv/peer_handler_reorg_test.dart`, rewritten `test/actors/header_sync_actor_test.dart` (real-PoW regtest chains). Mutation-verified: post-reorg batch stored at heights 5/6 instead of 3/4 (`Expected: 4, Actual: 6`), 2-block reorg did not replace the tip, restart tip `Expected: 4, Actual: 3`, no locator re-request. |

### SPV-04 · security · Fixed (post-2.0.0)
**CDN header sync trusts the CDN entirely**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `cdn_header_sync_service.dart:91, 147-148, 195-209`; `cdn_manifest.dart:50, 78-80`; `cdn_header_sync_config.dart:54` |
| Failure | Expected hashes and "checkpoints" come from the manifest on the same origin; the first chunk on an empty database is unanchored; PoW validation defaults off; `baseUrl` need not be https; `bulkImportHeaders` writes without validation. |
| Fix | First chunk on an empty database must start at height 0 with the `NetworkParams` genesis hash; otherwise it must link to the stored tip. Manifest checkpoints are advisory. Proof of work validated by default (bits at or below `powLimit`, hash at or below target). `allowInsecureHttp` (default false): http base URLs throw. Genesis chunk no longer skipped on an empty database. |
| Test | `test/spv/cdn_header_sync_service_test.dart` group "anchoring and proof of work (SPV-04)". Mutation-verified: nine cases, e.g. fake genesis accepted (`Expected: false, Actual: true`), genesis chunk skipped (`Expected: 10, Actual: 9`), http accepted. |

### SPV-05 · security · Fixed
**Path traversal: manifest-supplied chunk filenames were joined into the wallet data directory**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `cdn_header_sync_service.dart:224, 256, 274` |
| Fix | Filenames containing separators, `..` or NUL are rejected before use. |
| Test | `test/spv/cdn_header_sync_service_test.dart`: eight unsafe names rejected before any I/O, plain names resolve under the cache dir, and an end-to-end `synchronize()` with a manifest naming `../../escaped.bin` writes nothing outside the cache directory (`cacheFilePath` is `@visibleForTesting`). Mutation-verified: the escaped file was written two directories above the cache dir. |

### SPV-06 · correctness · Fixed (post-2.0.0)
**Library-built BUMPs are not BRC-74 compliant (two incompatible internal layouts)**

| | |
|---|---|
| File | `utils/tsc_converter.dart:105-114`; `utils/crypto_utils.dart:685-757`; `wallet_projection.dart:820-843` |
| Failure | One builder emits level 0 as the sibling only, the other as the txid only with siblings pushed up a level; the projection's proof store assumes the first layout. Outgoing BEEFs are rejected by standard verifiers; a real BRC-74 BUMP from ARC would be mis-stored. Masked because the local root computation tolerates both. |
| Fix | One BRC-74 builder (`BUMP.fromMerklePath` / `fromTscProof` / `merge`) used by `TscConverter` and every `CryptoUtils` builder; the projection stores the raw BUMP hex with the txid's level-0 offset as position and refuses proofs that do not contain the txid; the tolerant dual-layout parsing is gone. Library BUMPs are byte-identical to the real ARC BUMP fixture. |
| Test | `test/bump_brc74_test.dart` group "SPV-06" (self-constructed 8/5/3-leaf blocks with independently computed roots; real ARC BUMP cross-check); `test/projections/wallet_projection_merkle_proof_test.dart`. Mutation-verified: TSC-built bytes differed from ARC's; two-txid BUMP gave different roots; wrong stored position. |

### SPV-11 · security · Fixed (post-2.0.0)
**ARC client had no HTTP timeouts, calls endpoints that do not exist, parses a policy schema ARC does not return**

| | |
|---|---|
| Done | Every request is bounded by `ArcServiceConfig.requestTimeout` (default 30 s). |
| Done later | Shapes from `bitcoin-sv/arc` `pkg/api/arc.yaml`: merkle proofs read `merklePath` from `GET /v1/tx/{txid}`; the fake proof endpoints are gone; `ArcPolicyResponse` parses `policy.miningFee {satoshis, bytes}` and throws without it; `estimateFee` rounds up. Batch submit posts to `/txs` (was the non-existent `/tx/batch`). |
| Test | `test/services/arc_service_test.dart` group "request timeouts (SPV-11)": a never-responding client fails `submitTransaction` and `getTransaction` with `TimeoutException(200 ms)`. Mutation-verified: the 3 s guard fired instead (`Future not completed`). |
| Test (later) | `arc_service_test.dart` group "real ARC API shapes (SPV-11)". Mutation-verified: old code requested `/tx/<txid>/proof`, fee `0.5` instead of `50`, batch submit to `/tx/batch`. |

Follow-up review of every Isar query (libspiffy-ei4): `(walletId, purpose)` and `(status, walletId)` indexes replace `walletId` / `status`; `checkAddresses`, invoices, `listDeferredPayments` (filters, cursor and limit inside Isar) and `getUTXOsByPlugin` no longer read other wallets' rows or whole ranges. **Purging spent UTXOs: rejected by design** (see note below) | contract "S-16: transaction history pagination is exact at the boundaries"; `test/storage/isar_query_access_test.dart` replays each query through the `IsarWalletStorage.onQuery` seam and counts rows in range and rows read (old: `watch addresses Expected: [2] Actual: [52]`, `checkAddresses ... reads 3 row(s) of another wallet`; pre-wave-3 queries restored: `history page Expected: <5> Actual: <40>`); `isar_index_migration_test.dart` |

**Open**

None.

**Rejected by design (S-16, M7).** Both findings suggested purging spent UTXOs (and M7 dropping transaction history from aggregate state). libspiffy follows the peer-to-peer SPV model in `spv-understanding.md`: counterparties hand the wallet transactions with merkle proofs, the wallet never scans blocks, and a future BEEF needs ancestor transactions and their proofs. Data dropped locally cannot be fetched again, so the wallet keeps full transaction history, every spent UTXO with its spending history, and a proof for every transaction. Only the cost was fixed (indexes, paging, incremental balances); no maintenance path deletes history.

---

## 9. What was checked and found sound

| Area | Verified |
|---|---|
| Cryptographic primitives | AES-256-GCM with CSPRNG nonces and verified tags; HKDF-bound row contexts; RFC 6979 low-S ECDSA via dartsv; `Random.secure()` everywhere; `SIGHASH_ALL\|FORKID` at every signing site |
| BRC-42 (type-42) | Matches the specification and its ten published test vectors; public and private derivation agree; infinity and zero-scalar cases rejected |
| Key parsing | WIF/xpriv checksums, network prefixes, compression byte and zero keys rejected; `PostgresSecureStorage` refuses every private-key key name |
| Secret hygiene | No mnemonic, WIF, xpriv, private key or passphrase in any event, snapshot, command `toMap`, `toString`, log or exception |
| Event sourcing | Every emitted event type registered; `fromMap` accepts `String` and `DateTime`; BigInt as strings; `persistEvents` journals before state mutates; invoice and channel apply functions pure; version arithmetic consistent |
| Actor discipline | `context.sender` stable across awaits under dactor 1.3; temporary receivers stopped; timers cancelled in `postStop`; projections stopped before the event store closes; no dependence on ask retries |
| SQL and types | All SQL parameterised; BYTEA and TIMESTAMPTZ handled correctly; satoshis fit `BIGINT`; credentials never logged; `ANY(@addresses)` encoded as an array |
| Untrusted input | BEEF/BUMP parsing terminates and bounds allocation; CDN chunk hashes verified before parsing and cached chunks re-verified; WhatsOnChain retries rate-limited; node-RPC credentials only in the Authorization header |
| Transaction building | Fee rounding with a 1-sat floor; dust handling; P2MS key ordering consistent; funding and multisig spends run through the interpreter; channel requires `refundSigned` before open |

---

## 10. Pre-existing test failures

Bead **T-1** (libspiffy-hhj) lists every test that fails identically on the
pre-upgrade baseline. None is a regression. All are resolved (the last 10 in the follow-up batch, V-18); one of them hid a library defect.

| File | Tests | Cause | Status |
|---|---:|---|---|
| `test/services/transaction_builder_service_test.dart` | 17 | `ScriptTypeRegistry` cannot be re-initialised across tests; the service is dead code | Resolved: deleted with the service (SPV-12) |
| `test/core_models/bitcoin_utxo_test.dart` | 3 | Expects factory default status `available`; the model creates `pending` | Resolved: stale expectations updated (wave 2) |
| `test/core_models/bitcoin_transaction_test.dart` | 1 | Expects 6 `TransactionStatus` values; there are 8 | Resolved (wave 2) |
| `test/storage/libspiffy_schemas_test.dart` | 2 | Entity status default; `netAmount` late-init | Resolved: test bugs fixed (wave 3) |
| `test/beef_test.dart`, `test/conversion_test.dart` | 2 | Stale merkle/header expectations | Resolved (SPV-06/07) |
| `test/crypto_service_test.dart` | 2 | BIP44 derivation expectations | Resolved (H3) |
| `test/integration/benford_split_test.dart` | 3 | The helper never created the wallet (`CreateWalletCommand` sent as a wallet command), scripts were ASM text instead of hex, and split outputs are `available` after SEEN_ON_NETWORK since V-16 | Resolved: test fixed (V-18) |
| `test/integration/payment_api_test.dart`, `transaction_history_recordkeeping_test.dart` | 4 | Not races: `p2p_test_helpers` stored a header whose merkle root was a TSC node and a proof with byte-reversed nodes; funding UTXOs were left `pending`; the tests expected an unmined payment `confirmed`; the recipient wallet did not own the paid address | Resolved: real testnet header and BUMP, corrected expectations, the recipient broadcasts (V-18) |
| `test/integration/xpub_wallet_lifecycle_test.dart` | 1 | Race: the Benford coordinator read the wallet before `WalletCreatedEvent` was projected | Resolved: the test waits for the event (V-18) |
| `test/integration/payment_protocol_flow_test.dart` | 2 | Library defect V-18: ARC's deferred spend of a payment reserved under a payment id was rejected. The test also expected the input `spent` before anyone broadcast | Resolved: aggregate fix; the test expects `reserved`, then `spent` after the recipient broadcasts (V-18) |

Isar's native library also segfaults on process exit under `dart test` on
both trees; this does not affect results.

## 11. Defects found while verifying the fixes

Found after release 2.0.0 while writing the mutation-verified tests (V-1 to
V-5), during backlog wave 3 (V-6, V-7) and in the follow-up batch before
wave 4 (V-8 to V-34), wave 4 itself (V-35 to V-39) the follow-up beads wave 4 filed (V-40 to V-43) the P3 correctness wave after it (V-44 to V-51), that wave's follow-ups (V-52 to V-55) the SPV settlement fix after them (V-56) the SPV proof-lifecycle wave (V-57 to V-62) and the retention and re-proof wave (V-63 to V-67). All are fixed on `main` and
covered by the same fail-then-pass protocol.

| Id | Severity | Title | File | What was wrong | Test (mutation-verified) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| V-1 | High | Mainnet wallets could not sign after output scanning and threw on imported transactions | `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart` `_handleSignTransaction`; `wallet_projection.dart` `_createTransactionAddressJunctions` | `ScriptTypeRegistry` is a process-wide singleton pinned to the first network it is built with. Both sites constructed it with the testnet default, so once anything had pinned MAIN, `SignTransactionCommand` failed with `already initialized with NetworkType.MAIN, cannot reinitialize with NetworkType.TEST`, and `_handleTransactionImported` threw out of `handle()`. Both now pass `NetworkName.toDartsv(<wallet network>)`. | `test/core/bitcoin_wallet_aggregate_mainnet_test.dart` "mainnet wallet can sign after its outputs have been scanned"; `test/projections/wallet_projection_test.dart` "creates transaction-address junctions for an imported mainnet transaction" |
| V-2 | High | The A-M9 fix and nine other sites still compared the network string to the literal `'main'` | `libspiffy_actor_system.dart` (ARC default, CDN network, P2P network, default peers); `import_actor.dart` (7 sites); `address_discovery_service.dart` (2 sites) | `networkType: 'mainnet'` selected the testnet ARC endpoint, the testnet CDN, testnet P2P magic and testnet seeds, and derived testnet keys in the importer and discovery service. All now go through `NetworkName`. | `test/actors/arc_config_network_test.dart` "networkType 'mainnet' is treated like 'main'" (`Expected: arc.taal.com, Actual: arc-test.taal.com` before the fix); importer and discovery sites are exercised by `import_actor_network_test.dart` and `address_discovery_service_test.dart` |
| V-3 | Medium | An address generated without a label could not be signed for through the state lookup | `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart` `_getPrivateKeyForAddress` | `WalletState.addresses` maps address to an optional label; the lookup tested the value for null instead of the key for presence, so an unlabelled address raised `Address ... not found in wallet state`. Now `containsKey`. | `test/bitcoin_wallet_aggregate_test.dart` "signs for an address that was generated without a label" (`Bad state: Address n4gR... not found in wallet state` before the fix) |
| V-4 | Low | The Postgres suite was not re-runnable after the S-03 test | `test/storage/postgres/postgres_integration_test.dart` | The uint32-nonce test left block 1 in `block_headers`; v003's `down` could not narrow the column back to `INTEGER`, so the migration tests failed on every subsequent run with `22003: integer out of range`. The test file now clears `block_headers` before resets. | Suite passes on repeated runs (26 / 26) |
| V-5 | Low | The payment coordinator's reservation timeout was untestable | `payment_coordinator_actor.dart` | `_reservationReplyTimeout` was a private constant, so the A-H4 "silence is failure" path could only be tested with a 10 s wait. It is now the constructor parameter `reservationReplyTimeout` (default unchanged). | `test/actors/payment_coordinator_reservation_test.dart` |
| V-6 | Medium | `PostgresMigrations.reset()` / `rollback()` failed on a fresh database; `getCurrentVersion()` reported an unreachable database as unmigrated | `postgres_migrations.dart` | `rollback()` queried `schema_migrations` without checking it exists (`42P01: relation "schema_migrations" does not exist`); `getCurrentVersion` / `getAppliedMigrations` caught every error and returned 0 / `[]`, so a connection failure looked like an empty database and a migrate would run against it. Both fixed in wave 3. | `postgres_event_store_audit_test.dart` "reset() and rollback() on a database without schema_migrations", "getCurrentVersion reports an unreachable database instead of 0" (old: `42P01`; `emitted <0>`) |
| V-7 | High | Updates silently overwrote stored history in the read models | `in_memory_wallet_storage.dart`, `isar_wallet_storage.dart`, `postgres_wallet_storage.dart` | Found by the wave 3 retention audit (`spv-understanding.md`): a transaction update with an empty `rawHex` wiped the stored raw bytes on every backend (Postgres `COALESCE('', ...)` did not protect it); a confirmed update without `blockHeight` erased the stored height on Isar and in-memory (a non-confirmed update still clears it, so a reorg can take the confirmation back, 3b0); Postgres wrote `spent_in_tx_id = NULL` and nulled `plugin_metadata` on every update without them; Isar `toDomain` replaced `createdAt` with the read time. All fixed. | `wallet_lifecycle_contract.dart` "retention: an update without raw hex or block height keeps the stored ones" and "retention: a spent UTXO row stays after later updates" (old: `Expected: '0200000001deadbeef' Actual: ''`) |
| V-8 | High | Channel rejections reported as success; a rejected command stopped the channel aggregate | `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart` | After M10 the aggregate rejected invalid payment acknowledgements, payment records and refund countersignatures, but the manager replied `success: true` on those three paths. Every rejection also stopped the aggregate actor (eventador rethrows after replying; dactor stops unsupervised actors), and the manager kept the dead ref, so the next operation failed "Actor ... is not alive". All manager -> aggregate commands now go through one checked path; dead refs are replaced (libspiffy-lhd). | `test/actors/payment_channel_manager_rejection_test.dart` (old: `Expected: false / Actual: <true>`; second acceptance timed out) |
| V-9 | High | Postgres journal readers could skip events that committed out of id order | `postgres_event_store.dart` | Journal ids are allocated at INSERT; concurrent writers commit in any order, so keyset replay, replay-then-live and `eventsByTag` could pass a lower id that committed later and never deliver it; live streams saw no appends from other processes. v008 adds the writing transaction id; readers take only rows below `pg_snapshot_xmin` in `(tx_id, id)` order; live streams read from their own cursor and poll (libspiffy-xp4). | `test/storage/postgres/postgres_journal_order_test.dart` (old: `Actual: [(b-..., 353)]`, id 352 skipped; another store instance received `[]`) |
| V-10 | Medium | A reorg deleted the orphaned merkle proof from the read model | read-model storage, `wallet_projection.dart`, `spv_actor.dart` | Retention (`spv-understanding.md`): proofs now carry `verified` / `pendingHeader` / `orphaned`; nothing deletes a proof row; the `'pending'` block-hash placeholder is gone; BEEFs use only the current proof. v009 (libspiffy-mny). | `test/storage/merkle_proof_retention_contract.dart` on all three backends; old behaviour re-injected as mutants fails it (`Expected: an object with length of <1> Actual: []`) |
| V-11 | High | Any rejected command stopped the wallet and invoice aggregates | `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart`, `invoice_aggregate.dart`, managers | Same mechanism as V-8 for every aggregate: the next command for that wallet or invoice failed or timed out. A shared mixin keeps the actor running after a rejection (no journal recovery, so a peer cannot force replays) and stops it before replying when a journal write failed; managers replace dead refs; invoice failures were answered twice (libspiffy-201). | `test/actors/aggregate_command_rejection_test.dart` (old: `no matching AddressGeneratedResponse within 0:00:05`; `Expected: InvoiceStatus.paid Actual: InvoiceStatus.pending`) |
| V-12 | High | Client-side channel open stalled; the adapter used the last-created wallet | `channel_p2p_adapter.dart` | The server cast an absent `clientSignatureHex` to `String` on `refund_sign_request`, threw, and never countersigned. Inputs stayed reserved (and could return to `available` when the 30-minute reservation expired while the transaction was in the mempool); change, including channel funding change, was not credited until ARC reported MINED with a proof checked against a stored header. One deferred-spend path now runs on every on-network report (single broadcast, BEEF broadcast, the durable retry queue, every SEEN_ON_NETWORK and MINED scan report), driven by the UTXOs the read model still shows outstanding and deduplicated while the read model catches up; a MINED submit answer carrying a merkle path is checked and confirmed like a MINED status report. Rejected, double-spend, orphan and in-flight answers spend nothing (libspiffy-09k). | `test/actors/arc_actor_submit_deferred_spend_test.dart` (8 tests; old: `Expected: ['6af69a37…:0>a05924fc…'] Actual: []`); `channel_open_coordinator_e2e_test.dart` back on a SEEN_ON_NETWORK submit (old: `balance 0, expected 199899989`) |

The client now sends the funding transaction as BEEF (built with `AncestorChainService`, journaled in `ChannelOpenedEvent`); the server validates it with `SPVActor` like a received payment (BUMPs against our headers, ancestors, input scripts) plus an input/output value check, and refuses `channel_open` otherwise (libspiffy-fsy). | `channel_open_coordinator_e2e_test.dart` group "the server SPV-validates the funding transaction": missing unproven ancestor, merkle root mismatch, bad input script, outputs above inputs, no BEEF (old: `Expected: <Instance of 'ErrorEvent'> Actual: <Instance of 'ChannelOpenedEvent'>`) |
| V-21 | Medium | Channel open state was lost on restart; an interrupted funding broadcast was recorded twice; the channel peer id was never set | `channel_p2p_adapter.dart`, `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart`, `libspiffy_actor_system.dart` | The adapter kept channel, peer and pending-request state in memory only, so a restart mid-open stalled the channel; a resumed broadcast re-recorded the funding in the wallet; `LibSpiffyActorSystem` never passed a peer id, so requests carried `clientPeerId: ''`. The adapter now rebuilds from the channel journal (`ChannelDetailsQueryMessage`), `FundingRecordedInWalletEvent` marks the wallet record, and `initialize(channelPeerId:)` is journaled with `ChannelAcceptedEvent.serverPeerId` (libspiffy-fsy, libspiffy-36f). | `channel_open_coordinator_e2e_test.dart` group "channel state survives a restart", 7 tests (old: `Channel open stalled.`; `Expected: <1> Actual: <2> the resumed broadcast recorded the funding transaction in the wallet again`) |
| V-22 | High | 1 in 256 HD child keys could not be derived, so wallets handed out addresses they could not sign for | `dartsv_crypto_service.dart`, `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart`, `address_discovery_service.dart` | dartsv 3.0.0 `HDPrivateKey._deriveChildPrivateKey` copies the minimal-length child key into a 32-byte slot and throws `Bad state: Too few elements` when it starts with a zero byte; public derivation does not, so the address exists but its key cannot be derived (a short m/0 or m/1 makes every receive or change address unsignable). `lib/src/utils/bip32.dart` derives with fixed-width keys; for every key dartsv could derive, results are bit-identical (BIP32 vectors 1-4, seeded comparison with an independent implementation), so existing wallets keep their addresses (libspiffy-hvp). | `test/crypto/hd_derivation_leading_zero_test.dart` (old: `Bad state: Too few elements` at `hdprivatekey.dart 201:19`), `test/crypto/bip32_test.dart`; `crypto_service_test.dart` and `payment_channel_aggregate_actor_test.dart` made deterministic |
| V-23 | Medium | Multisig invoice payments were not recognised, and an invoice multisig output could be credited as balance | `spv_actor.dart`, `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart`, `invoice_aggregate.dart` | SPVActor read the multisig keys under `pubKeys` (dartsv names them `publicKeys`), so an invoice paid to its multisig output was never marked paid and a mixed invoice was refused as underpaid; the aggregate exempted outputs under the `p2ms:m-of-n` pseudo-address from the V-17 rule, so once read they were credited whatever keys the wallet held. Invoice multisig outputs now count towards the invoice; they are wallet UTXOs only when the wallet holds at least m keys; `InvoiceAggregate` accepts a payment to its multisig output (libspiffy-n0p). | `test/integration/invoice_multisig_payment_test.dart` (old: `Timed out waiting for: invoice marked paid`; `Payment amount (40000 sats) is less than invoice amount (100000 sats)`), `test/core/invoice_multisig_paid_test.dart`, `wallet_output_ownership_test.dart` n0p group |
| V-24 | High | A merkle proof contradicting the stored header was kept as pending and used in BEEFs and confirmations | `wallet_projection.dart`, `ancestor_chain_service.dart`, `spv_actor.dart`, read-model storage | A proof whose root did not match the header at its height was stored as `pendingHeader`, stayed current, went into BEEFs our own SPV check rejects, and backed a confirmation until the next header batch. New status `rejected` (row kept, never current, never in a BEEF); a confirmation resting only on a rejected proof is reverted through the journal and ARC is asked for a real proof; BEEFs use verified proofs and pendingHeader proofs only while no header exists at their height. Postgres v012 (libspiffy-azl). | `wallet_projection_proof_status_test.dart` azl group (old: `Expected: null Actual: <Instance of 'MerkleProof'>`), `ancestor_chain_beef_order_test.dart` (old: `Ancestor transaction at index 0 failed merkle proof validation`), `reorg_confirmation_revert_test.dart`, `spv_rejected_proof_revert_test.dart`, `merkle_proof_retention_contract.dart` azl rows |
| V-25 | High | A handed-over payment's inputs were released after 2 minutes, so the wallet could double-spend its own payment | `payment_coordinator_actor.dart`, `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart`, `arc_actor.dart`, `wallet_manager_actor.dart` | In the peer-to-peer model the recipient broadcasts, possibly much later; the payment reservation expired after 2 minutes and the cleanup returned the inputs to `available`. A REJECTED answer only marked the row failed, journaling nothing. Decision (user): a deferred payment's inputs are held by the wallet aggregate (`wallet.transaction.spend_deferred`) until the network reports it (spend applies), ARC rejects it definitively (`deferred_failed`, inputs released), or the user cancels it (`deferred_cancelled`, refused if the network knows the transaction); expiry, cleanup and higher-priority reservations cannot free them; journals from before are held by inference on load. New coordinator API: `GetDeferredPaymentsQuery`, `BroadcastDeferredPaymentCommand`, `CheckDeferredPaymentStatusCommand`, `CancelDeferredPaymentCommand`, ARC or a blockchain data source (a MINED answer confirms only with a proof matching our headers). Postgres v013 (libspiffy-7p2). | `test/integration/deferred_payment_hold_test.dart` (old: `Expected: not contains 'a05924fc…:1' Actual: ['a05924fc…:1']`; `Expected: UTXOStatus.reserved Actual: UTXOStatus.available`), plus aggregate, projection, ARC actor, storage contract (3 backends), Postgres and coordinator end-to-end tests proven by mutants |
| V-26 | High | A valid payment to a freshly created wallet or a just-generated address could be validated but not credited | `spv_actor.dart`, `wallet_manager_actor.dart`, `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart`, `wallet_messages.dart` | SPVActor decided which outputs (addresses, multisig keys, plugin owner addresses) and spent inputs belonged to the target wallet by reading the read model, which lags the wallet's journal. A BEEF arriving before the projection applied `WalletCreatedEvent` / `AddressGeneratedEvent` returned `isValid: true` with no spendable UTXOs, so nothing was credited while the payer believed it had paid (the recordkeeping integration test failed 7 of 12 runs). SPVActor now sends a `WalletOwnershipQuery` through WalletManagerActor to the wallet aggregate, which answers from its event-sourced state; its FIFO mailbox means every address generation already acknowledged is known. Watch addresses, which only the read model records, are added from the read model (additive only). A wallet that does not exist or does not answer within 30 s now fails the result (`isValid: false`) instead of validating with its outputs dropped. The received transaction, raw hex and proof are recorded as before (libspiffy-29t). | `test/actors/spv_attribution_read_model_lag_test.dart`, run with no projection (old: `Expected: [(int, String):(0, mmwDj96k…)] Actual: []` for a generated address, a multisig key and a new wallet's receive and spend; `Expected: false Actual: <true>` for an unknown wallet); the projection wait in `transaction_history_recordkeeping_test.dart` is removed (8 / 8 whole-file runs) |
| V-27 | High | P2PK outputs received through SPV were never credited | `spv_actor.dart` `_decodeOutputLock` | The P2PK branch read `scriptInfo['pubKey']`; dartsv names it `publicKey` (an `SVPublicKey`), so no P2PK output ever got an address and none was attributed to the wallet (same class as the dead `pubKeys` read of V-23). It now reads `publicKey` (object or hex) and attributes the key's P2PKH address (libspiffy-4fq). | `test/actors/spv_receive_output_reading_test.dart` group `libspiffy-4fq` (old: `Expected: [(int, String, String):(0, mmwDj96k…, P2PK)] Actual: []`) |
| V-28 | High | An invoice payment the invoice path could not check validated with nothing credited | `spv_actor.dart` `_validateReceivedTransaction` | With an invoice id, an unanswered invoice lookup, an unknown invoice, or a found invoice that no output paid returned empty output lists, skipped the amount check and returned `isValid: true`: nothing was recorded, the invoice stayed pending, and the coordinator broadcast the BEEF and reported it valid. The invoice is now looked up once per receive; each of these cases fails the result, naming the invoice. `full_spv_validation_test` passed only because of this defect (mainnet helper address against a testnet actor, invoice amount above the payment); fixed (libspiffy-n8b9). | same file, group `libspiffy-n8b9` (old: `Expected: false Actual: <true>` for no answer, unknown invoice and an unpaid invoice) |
| V-29 | Medium | An output whose locking script threw while being read was dropped silently | `spv_actor.dart`, `wallet_messages.dart`, `coordinator_messages.dart` | A template or plugin exception while reading an output was logged and the output treated as nobody's, with the result valid. Failing the result would lose the whole transaction (the wallet manager ignores invalid results), so the outputs are listed in the new `SPVValidationResult.unreadableOutputs` / `SPVValidationResultEvent.unreadableOutputs` (vout, satoshis, script, reason) and the transaction is still recorded whole (libspiffy-rp6x). | same file, group `libspiffy-rp6x` (old: `Expected: an object with length of <1> Actual: []`) |
| V-30 | High | Bare multisig and P2PK wallet UTXOs were signed as P2PKH, so a payment spending one failed | `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart` `_handleSignTransaction`, channel funding; `payment_coordinator_actor.dart` | Since V-17 a bare multisig output the wallet can spend alone is a wallet UTXO, and since V-27 P2PK outputs are credited, but every input was unlocked as P2PKH: the interpreter check refused the signature and the payment failed. | V-56 | High | A merkle proof handed to us peer-to-peer never settled our own payment: a BEEF carrying the proof of a transaction this wallet recorded left its deferred payment unsettled and its change output pending | `spv_actor.dart`, `wallet_manager_actor.dart`, `wallet_messages.dart`, `outgoing_transactions.dart`, `utxo_ledger.dart`, `wallet_commands.dart` | A counterparty spending what we paid them hands back our own payment as a proven ancestor of their new transaction; that BUMP is the strongest evidence there is that our payment was mined, and it is how the peer-to-peer model settles. Nothing compared a BEEF member's txid against the wallet's own transactions: the proof was filed in the txid-keyed ancestor store and the payment stayed outstanding, marked `seen` at best by the weaker inference that its inputs were spent. `mined` was reachable only from `ConfirmTransactionCommand`, issued only by ARC's MINED report and by reorg revival, so only ARC could settle a payment. A proven subject also short-circuited the validation loop, discarding every other transaction and BUMP in the BEEF, and when the subject was a transaction we had recorded the read-model row flipped to `confirmed` while the aggregate never journaled the confirmation, leaving write and read models split until ARC caught up. One rule now holds: a BUMP that verifies against our active header chain confirms that txid if the wallet recorded it, wherever it sits in the BEEF (`SPVValidationResult.provenTransactions`, `ConfirmTransactionCommand.onlyIfRecorded`). Confirming also makes the transaction's own pending outputs available, so the change of a deferred payment is spendable from the moment the proof reaches us. A second receipt of an outpoint the wallet already holds is a no-op instead of a `StateError` nobody could observe (the senders `tell()` with no sender), and the stored row, with its reservation and spending history, is never overwritten (libspiffy-fggl). | `test/integration/beef_proof_confirms_recorded_test.dart` (old: `Timed out waiting for: the wallet journals the confirmation of its own payment`; `Expected: <1> Actual: <2>` without the idempotency guard; `Expected: UTXOStatus.available Actual: UTXOStatus.pending` for the change output; `Expected: empty Actual: [TransactionConfirmedEvent…] a forged proof confirmed our own payment`), `received_ancestor_retention_test.dart` (old: `a proven subject discarded the rest of the BEEF`), `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate_utxo_status_test.dart`, `aggregate_state_characterization_test.dart` |

The fee is ARC's published policy fee and nothing else: there is no replace-by-fee on this network, so a conflicting transaction cannot displace ours by paying more and ours cannot displace theirs. First seen wins, there is no race and no front-running, and the command has no fee parameter at all. The quote comes from a new `EstimatePolicyFeeMessage` rather than `EstimateFeeMessage`, which silently falls back to a guessed 1 sat/1000 bytes when ARC's policy cannot be read; the new quote fails instead and the reclaim refuses to build anything, so the wallet never invents a fee. The self-spend takes over the hold of the payment it reclaims (`TransactionSpendDeferredEvent.supersedes`), so the old payment is never left unheld, and the new terminal `reclaimed` state is reached only when the network has the self-spend, never at broadcast time. Cancel is refused for a payment being reclaimed and for a reclaim's own self-spend. Nothing is deleted: both payments keep their records, raw hex and held-input lists, linked by `purpose` and `resolutionReason`. The other is the counterparty who handed us the transaction supplying a fresh BEEF, and supplying the proofs an ancestry needs is the sender's obligation - not something we fetch from a service, since an ARC instance answers only for what was submitted through it. The obligation belongs to the sender of the payment, not the ancestor's own counterparty: the ancestors are usually strangers' transactions from the payment's history that we never dealt with. The peer to ask is therefore the one named by the `counterpartyMarker` on the row for the output's transaction (V-68), and `OutputAwaitingProof` now carries it. Transport stays the app's, following the `ChannelP2PAdapter` precedent exactly: `P2PMessageReceived` in, `P2PMessageToSendEvent` out, types `proof_request`/`proof_response` routed by `messageType`, with the channel-named classes now extending the generic bases unchanged. Both halves are built, because a protocol nobody answers cannot be tested and libspiffy is the wallet on both sides of a payment. The responder answers only a peer matching the marker recorded on one of our rows for that transaction - answering anyone would tell any peer which transactions the wallet knows - and the requester likewise accepts a response only from the peer recorded for that txid, so an unsolicited BEEF never reaches the receive path on the strength of our having asked. Every refusal reads identically on the wire; the real reason is emitted locally only. A response is verified against our own header chain through the ordinary receive path (no second verifier); one that does not verify is rejected and the output stays awaiting, while the BEEF is retained under V-64, and a fresh verified proof supersedes the orphaned row through the existing `planMerkleProofStore`. A request naming no identity is refused outright, and the marker comparison ignores rows recording no marker so an empty peer id cannot match an absent one - that second check is unreachable while the first guard stands and is defence against a later edit, not a separately tested behaviour (libspiffy-a2v3). | `test/integration/counterparty_proof_request_test.dart` (5 tests, two real actor systems on separate Isar databases, regtest chains, messages JSON round-tripped between coordinators as an app's transport would; with an unverified response reported as success: `Expected: false Actual: <true>`; with the response-side marker check dropped: the same on `an unsolicited proof response … is refused, however good its proof`), `test/actors/proof_request_anonymous_requester_test.dart` (with the unnamed-peer guard removed: `a request from a peer that names no identity must not be processed at all`) |

That is precisely what V-60 exists to prevent (a height a caller hands us is a claim, not evidence), and it also un-voided an output that V-73 designed to be revivable only by a proof that outranks the resolution. `updateConfirmations` now records the count and the height and touches neither `status` nor `statusBeforeReservation`, closing three promotions rather than one: pending to available, voided to available, and the reserved case where a count silently changed what a later release would restore. The command is **deprecated** rather than kept alive with validation: nothing in `lib/` sends it (only tests did), V-71 had already decided the wallet derives a confirmation count instead of storing one, and V-60 that a caller's height is not evidence, so it has nothing left to do that is both correct and needed; the only would-be sender is a code sketch in `wallet-architecture.md` with a placeholder wallet id, while the real `ARCActor` uses `MarkUTXOAvailableCommand` and `ConfirmTransactionCommand`. Deprecated, not deleted - it is public API via `internals.dart`. `blockHeight` is now nullable on the event and the model, so an absent height is recorded as absent rather than journaled as 0, the genesis block, and a command carrying no height no longer erases a height a proof established. Backwards compatibility was deliberately not preserved (pre-release, no journals worth keeping, owner's decision): the promotion is gone from the apply path outright rather than hidden behind a discriminator that would keep old events promoting. One characterization test had pinned the promotion on purpose and that decision is reversed here deliberately - it kept passing only because `WalletBalances` buckets on the count over all unspent UTXOs, so its balance assertion never saw the status (libspiffy-8oaq). | `test/core/confirmation_update_spendability_test.dart` (8 tests, each checked against both live aggregate state and a fresh journal replay; old: `Expected: UTXOStatus:<pending> Actual: UTXOStatus:<available>`, the same for `voided`, the same again on release of a reserved pending UTXO, and `Expected: null Actual: <0>` / `Expected: <910000> Actual: <0>` for the genesis-block default), plus `test/core_models/bitcoin_utxo_test.dart` and `test/projections/wallet_projection_test.dart` at the model and read-model levels |
| V-79 | Medium | Four definitions of "confirmed" and three different answers: the same output could be confirmed in one API and unconfirmed in another, and a proof-confirmed output read as unconfirmed until five more blocks arrived | `wallet_balances.dart`, `bitcoin_utxo.dart`, `bitcoin_transaction.dart`, `wallet_projection.dart`, `wallet_coordinator_actor.dart`, `utxo_ledger.dart`, `arc_actor.dart`, `wallet_manager_actor.dart` | The aggregate's buckets and the read model's wallet row used a stored count of six or more (`WalletBalances.confirmedAt`), `BalanceResponse` used "has a block height", and `BitcoinUtxo.isConfirmed` wanted a height AND a positive count. One rule now, stated in spv-understanding.md under "Balances": confirmed is evidenced by the transaction appearing in a block whose header we hold on our active chain. Since V-60 and V-78 a UTXO's `blockHeight` is written only by a confirmation verified against our own header chain, and a reorganization takes it off again, so `blockHeight != null` **is** that evidence and every layer answers it the same way; reserved still wins and voided still counts nowhere. There is deliberately no depth threshold - a proof confirms at depth one exactly as at depth six, and waiting for depth is an application's policy, not this library's. `confirmedAt` is deleted rather than deprecated: it is not on the public export surface, and a surviving constant keeps a count-based test readable and re-usable, so external code referencing it should fail to compile rather than silently keep the old rule. `BitcoinUtxo.updateConfirmations` lost its `blockHeight` parameter outright, so the deprecated `UpdateUTXOConfirmationsCommand` structurally cannot record a height (libspiffy-pq8p, the V-78 residue); the event still journals the claim but the apply gives it no authority, which is what makes the rule airtight now that `blockHeight` means confirmed. Fabricated counts removed: `WalletManagerActor`'s `confirmations: 1` on a proven receive, the projection's `1`/`6`, and ARCActor's `confirmations = blockHeight != null ? 6 : 0 // Simplified` - ARC answers with a status and a height and says nothing about depth. Five reads of a count survive and are each justified in the lane report: a revert guard that can only widen what a revert lowers, the three backends' shared rule that a zero-confirmation update clears a stored height (protective: it stops a heightless write dropping a proven height), and one external RPC source's own metadata that never reaches a wallet row. A real bug surfaced on the way: the projection stamped a proven height without recomputing balances - deliberately, because the height used to touch no bucket - so under the new rule the wallet row lagged the aggregate after a confirmation (libspiffy-jc3h, libspiffy-pq8p). | `test/core/confirmed_means_proven_height_test.dart` (16 tests, each applying one hand-written journal to both the aggregate and a real `WalletProjection` so both layers are asserted from the same events; old: `Expected: BalanceBucket:<confirmed> Actual: BalanceBucket:<unconfirmed>` at depth one, `Expected: BalanceBucket:<unconfirmed> Actual: <confirmed>` for a deep count with no height, `Expected: null Actual: <900000>` for the reported height, and `Expected: BigInt:<40000> Actual: BigInt:<0>` for the projection lag), plus updated assertions in `wallet_collaborators_test.dart` (whose test was named "the 6-confirmation boundary" and had pinned the threshold deliberately), `wallet_state_test.dart`, `bitcoin_utxo_test.dart`, `bitcoin_transaction_test.dart`, `wallet_projection_test.dart`, `wallet_projection_utxo_load_test.dart`, `wallet_available_balance_test.dart` and both characterization tests |

This is the defect V-78 and V-79 fixed for UTXO rows, still present one level up for transactions - and since V-79 the transaction row's height **is** the confirmed test for transactions, so an invented height is no longer inert. It was held harmless only by a guard at a distance (the read model and the receive path write a height onto a row only when `bumpProof` is non-empty), which is the wrong reason for a rule to hold: an absence must be representable. The height is now nullable on the event and on `RecordImportedTransactionCommand` (still `required`, so every caller states it), both `?? 0` in `SPVActor` are gone, `WalletManagerActor` reads `as int?`, and the aggregate writes no `blockHeight` key at all when there is none - the same shape `applyConfirmationReverted` leaves behind. One behaviour change beyond the absence: a re-delivery carrying no proof no longer takes away a height an earlier proof established, which previously happened for any record that had not yet reached `status: confirmed` (73bj guarded only confirmed records, and the old value 0 also *lowered* the height rather than clearing it). Backwards compatibility deliberately not preserved (pre-release, no journals worth keeping, owner's standing decision) (libspiffy-nys0). | `test/actors/unproven_import_has_no_height_test.dart` and `test/core/imported_transaction_height_test.dart` (10 tests; old: `Expected: null Actual: <0>` from `SPVActor` and from the journaled event, `Expected: false Actual: <true>` for the height key on the record, and `Expected: <1239645> Actual: <null>` for the re-delivery). It now lands in the existing `settlementTxId` column rather than a new one: exactly one transaction can ever spend the 2-of-2 funding output (BSV, first seen wins, a mined spend cannot be replaced), so a channel has exactly one closing txid and `state` already distinguishes the cooperative route (`closed`) from the refund route (`expired`). The receive path always tells the WalletManager its verdict, which then logged and dropped an untargeted result on every channel open; using a receive for a question that is not a receive is the defect and the log line only the symptom. The existing `ValidateBEEFMessage` was checked first and is not the same thing: it only calls `beef.validate()`, a structural check that never verifies a BUMP against our header chain, so switching to it would have turned a real SPV check into a parse check. New internal `ValidateCounterpartyTransactionMessage` runs the same `_validateReceivedTransaction` and replies to the sender only: nothing is credited, and nothing is parked — a caller asking a question is answered now. Retention is unchanged: the BEEF's transactions and proofs are still filed, because nothing can hand them to us again. The parent then found that the answer for a missing header still said the receive "is retried automatically once the headers arrive", which is untrue on a path that never parks; the promise is now conditional on there actually being a retry. (b) `RecordPaymentCommand` had no positivity guard, and every other guard is inert against a negative amount: the insufficient-balance check cannot trigger, and the arithmetic *raises* the client balance and lowers the server's, so a client could journal a payment that clawed back what it had already paid. (c) The adapter reported a refused or failed channel step as a local `ErrorEvent` only — no `channel_error` ever went on the wire, though the inbound half has always existed — so a counterparty waited for a handshake message that was never coming. Fixed upstream instead: eventador gains an optional `AwaitEventApplied(awaitId:)` and a `CancelEventAwait(awaitId)` that drops the registrations carrying it, cancels their timers and answers each `AwaitFailed(reason: 'cancelled')`, so a caller waiting on the original `ask` is released at once rather than at the timeout (additive; a registration without an id behaves exactly as before). `_awaitApplied` now returns a handle - the future and a `cancel()` - and both catch blocks call it. **libspiffy builds against a local eventador checkout until that release is published** (`dependency_overrides` in `pubspec.yaml`, bead libspiffy-ppl5). The hand-rolled one left out what it had not thought of: **plugin-managed outputs**. A token or a funding earmark could therefore be selected as ordinary funding and consumed — destroying the token, since the output is gone and the plugin's state no longer has one behind it.
