The wallet recorded the money leaving and not the money coming back. **Recorded as a receive, not an outgoing payment**: the 2-of-2 funding output is not a wallet UTXO (bead libspiffy-viy), so a settlement spends no wallet input and only creates wallet outputs - the shape of the existing counterparty-receive path. An outgoing record would have been wrong twice: `WalletProjection._handleTransactionRecorded` hard-codes `netAmount = -(paymentAmount + fee)`, so the row would read as money leaving on the transaction that brings it back, and `TransactionRowRules.primaryCounterpartyOf` keys direction off that sign. Nothing is double-counted: the client's funding record already charged the funding amount and fee, so crediting only its share leaves a net of exactly what it paid the server plus fees; the server recorded nothing at funding and now records its share. The funding record's `recipientAddresses: ['channel:<id>']` is mirrored as the settlement's `sendingAddresses`, so the two halves read as one story and neither pretends a channel is an address. **Evidence:** no height, no proof, no ARC (we did not broadcast it) - the row is pending and its outputs are pending until a BUMP confirms them through the ordinary path. **Two defects surfaced.** (a) `_handlePaymentSignatureResponse` passed `fullySignedPaymentTxHex: ''` with the comment "Would need to combine sigs - simplified for now", so no side ever held a settlement and its txid was unknowable (an unsigned template's txid is not the signed transaction's); the server holds both signatures at acknowledgement, so they are now combined and checked against the funding output exactly as the refund path does, and an assembly that fails records nothing rather than inventing a transaction. | V-88 | High | A transaction's `nLockTime` read back as `0` on two of the three backends: not an absent reading, the opposite one | `bitcoin_transaction.dart`, `libspiffy_schemas.dart`, `postgres_wallet_storage.dart`, `in_memory_wallet_storage.dart`, `transaction_row_rules.dart`, `v023_transaction_lock_time_and_version.dart`, `payment_coordinator_actor.dart` | The write path carried both consensus fields correctly - `TransactionImportedEvent.txLockTime`/`txVersion`, put on the row by the projection - and then storage threw them away: neither the Isar entity nor the Postgres table had a column for either, so `toDomain()` invented them (`lockTime: 0, // Would need to parse from rawHex or store separately`; `lockTime: 0, // Not stored in DB, default to 0`). The record remembers the **last** delivery, not every one, so a delivery re-sent after a different one is journaled again rather than compared against a growing list of digests: the safe direction, a duplicate rather than a loss. The flow now runs the expiry path plus the one thing expiry deliberately does not do: **a broadcast**. Order is broadcast, then journal, then record - a claim is a claim about the network, so nothing the network refused is journaled as claimed. **There is no replace-by-fee on BSV**: a rejection (most likely the counterparty's settlement having got there first) is terminal, recorded honestly on the response, never retried at a higher fee. Postgres wrote `metadata_json = COALESCE(@metadataJson, wallet_metadata.metadata_json)`. **`COALESCE` preserves the old document only when the WHOLE incoming map is null**: any non-null map overwrote the entire JSON document. Blast radius was partly masked because three of the four `lib/` callers read-modify-write in Dart first; the exposed paths are `wallet_projection._handleWalletCreated`, which writes a fresh map with no spread — so on **replay** it wiped host entries and derived keys on Postgres, and `_recalculateAndPersistForWallet` restores balances, not host metadata — and any host calling the public `storeWallet` directly, which the documented contract tells them is safe with a partial map. It cannot — and could not on Isar or in-memory before either; only Postgres could, by accident. So no capability was knowingly removed, and the absence is now written down: a null value blanks a key and keeps it, and the whole document goes only with `deleteWallet`. **Why it survived**: `storeWallet` IS covered by contract tests on all three backends, but the merge rule was the one rule left duplicated per backend instead of shared — which is exactly why it diverged. | V-107 | High | A wallet whose account xpub was missing from secure storage could never derive another address, for the life of the wallet, although the key material the xpub comes from was sitting beside it | `wallet_keys.dart` | `generateAddress` read `wallet_hdpubkey_<id>` and threw a bare `StateError` when it was absent. There is no backfill anywhere — a grep for one in `lib/` finds only Postgres column backfills. **The bead's three-cohort premise is wrong, and the correction is the finding, for the seventh time in this backlog.** It claimed wallets created before `6e73584` have the xpub nowhere but the journal, and that wallets between `6e73584` and `edc0951` are "the genuine backfill-from-the-event cohort". Checked against the history instead of assumed: `wallet_hdpubkey_` is written at creation in `4fd3822`, **the first commit of the repository**, and every creating branch has written it ever since — the xpriv branch from `554fc38`, the xpub branch from `6e73584` (which also writes `wallet_xpub_`). `storeKeyMaterial` is the only writer of key material in all of `lib/`. So **no release ever created an HD wallet without the key**, and the journal-backfill cohort is empty; reading the deprecated event field would have been code for a case that does not exist. What remains is real and is what the fix addresses: the H4 window before `cb9e5b9`, where the event was journaled *before* the secrets were written so an interrupted creation leaves a wallet with no material at all, and any host that moves, restores or re-encrypts its secure storage and carries the seed across without the key derived from it. **The sharper framing is an inconsistency inside one class**: `privateKeyAtIndex` already walked xpriv → mnemonic, so such a wallet kept *signing* and could not *receive* — two methods needing the same key material, one with a fallback chain and one with a hard-coded read (AGENTS.md: "if three storage backends implement the same interface, they handle the same edge cases"). `accountXpub` now walks the same chain: `wallet_hdpubkey_`, then `wallet_xpub_`, then `wallet_xpriv_`, then `wallet_mnemonic_` with its passphrase. **The recovery is safe only because it is verified, which the bead does not raise at all.** A mnemonic wallet's xpub depends on its BIP39 passphrase, and a secure storage that lost the hdpubkey may have lost `wallet_passphrase_` too — in which case the mnemonic alone derives a **different wallet**, and returning that xpub would hand out addresses this wallet can never sign for and coin sent to them would be unspendable: far worse than the failure it replaces. So a recovered xpub is trusted only if it re-derives the wallet's journaled root address, `m/0/0`, **a path confirmed unchanged since `4fd3822`** so the check is sound across every journal. A mismatch is terminal rather than a fall-through to the next source: an absence is recoverable, a mismatch is a different wallet, and guessing on is how unspendable addresses get handed out. A verified recovery is written back so it happens once, and a failed write-back is logged rather than thrown because the address derives regardless. Where nothing can be recovered the error now names every key that was looked for and states that the existing addresses and their coin are untouched — a host can tell a lost secret from a lost derived key (libspiffy-atl2) | `test/core/wallet_account_xpub_recovery_test.dart` (7 tests). Seven mutations on the fixed code, each failing alone: the verification disabled, the mismatch made a fall-through instead of terminal, the write-back removed, each of the three fallback sources removed in turn, and the stored key ignored so an intact wallet takes the recovery path. The last is what pins that an intact watch-only wallet never starts depending on key material it does not hold |

| V-109 | Medium | The two halves of one payment protocol disagreed about what a valid payment is, and a channel's refund could be claimed twice with two different transactions | `payment_channel_aggregate.dart`, `channel_state.dart`, `payment_channel_messages.dart` | Payment-channel seam, pass 1 of the production-readiness sweep. **(1)** `_handleAcknowledgePayment` (server) guarded that both proposed balances are non-negative and sum to the funding amount; `_handleRecordPayment` (client) guarded neither. The client's own arithmetic made both true *inductively* — its expected balances are derived from the current ones, so the sum is preserved by construction — but that induction rests on the opening state being right, and **a rule two handlers enforce differently is a rule that will diverge**. Factored into one `_checkPaymentInvariants` both call, rather than copying the server's three checks into the client: the bead asked for one or the other and this is the one AGENTS.md's consistency rule points at. The client keeps its own `Insufficient balance` check ahead of the shared rule so it still names its own problem rather than the derived mismatch that follows from it. **A message was deliberately changed**: the two balance-mismatch errors became one naming both sides and both expectations, because a payment that moves the wrong amount usually has both balances wrong and naming only the first one checked made the answer depend on which handler asked. The default is now `WalletRowRules.defaultNetwork` = `NetworkName.canonical(null)`, and testnet is also the safe direction to be wrong in: a wallet wrongly taken for testnet cannot encode an address that receives real coins. **Wider than the bead, and the part that is not a default at all**: a store now **canonicalises** the network it is given (`WalletRowRules.canonicalNetwork`), so the `'main'`/`'test'`/`'regtest'` vocabulary the actor system, importer and P2P layer use — `ImportWalletCommand.networkType` defaults to `'test'` and is public — cannot land in a row beside the read model's `'mainnet'`/`'testnet'`. **The third spelling is reconciled, not renamed**: `'test'` is the P2P/dartsv vocabulary, not a fourth network, so what has to hold is that it resolves to the same network, and that is now pinned by a test that reads each layer's declared default rather than restating the answer. One load of everything is still unbounded: the rows are loaded `includeSpent: true`, spend history is never purged by design (`spv-understanding.md`, Data Retention), and so **the cost of every future event grew with every spend the wallet had ever made**. **The bead asked for the API to be decided before it was built, and reading the callers changed the answer.** It proposed a single-outpoint getter for ~13 projection handlers; but eleven of those twelve go on to call `_recalculateAndPersistForWallet`, which needs a SET, so a point lookup alone would have left the full load exactly where it was. What the recalculation actually wanted from the spend history was **two counts** (`spentUtxoCount`, and `utxoCount` over it) -- never the rows. So the API is three methods, not one: `getUTXO` (one outpoint, spent rows included, because a replayed spend or a refused reservation is a question about a row the wallet already spent), `getUTXOsByTxid` (one transaction's outputs), and `countSpentUTXOs`. `_UtxoRows` stopped being one eager load and became the rows a handler reads: the outpoint its event names, and -- only if it recomputes balances -- the UNSPENT set. **The bead's other two shapes were checked and one was already right**: the 'batch of outpoints' callers (`arc_actor.dart`, `wallet_coordinator_actor.dart`, `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart`) read `getUTXOs(walletId)`, which is unspent-only and bounded, and `arc_actor` already says why in a comment; they were left alone rather than converted on speculation. | V-115 | Medium | A receive replayed after a restart credited the wallet and told the app nothing | `spv_actor.dart`, `wallet_coordinator_actor.dart`, `internal_messages.dart` | V-63 made a parked receive durable so the header arriving after a restart still credits the wallet — nothing can ask a counterparty to send the BEEF again. What it could not carry across the restart is the **reply target**: an `ActorRef` dies with its process, `_parkedSenders` is in memory by design, and `_runReceive` told only `replyTo`. So the replay credited the wallet and **emitted no `CoordinatorEvent`**; an app restarted between the park and the header found the funds only by polling the read model. **The bead offered two designs and one of them does not work.** Having the projection emit for replayed receives cannot work, because a replayed receive journals exactly what a fresh one does — the projection has no way to tell them apart, so it would either duplicate the coordinator's own event or replace it. So: the coordinator registers itself with SPVActor (`SetCoordinatorForSPVMessage`, the shape `SetArcActorForSPVMessage` already uses), and SPVActor answers it whenever no caller is waiting. That covers the restart AND the `_maxParkedSenders` eviction, where a live process forgets a caller and today drops the answer with a log line. **Only a verdict is announced**: a receive that goes back to waiting for a header is not an outcome, and announcing one would tell an app its import failed about a receive that is fine — and tell it again on every header that does not settle it. **A second defect, found while fixing the first, and the reason the fix is not only a reference.** SPVActor replayed the receives whose headers arrived while the node was down in its own `preStart`, which runs BEFORE the coordinator exists — so for that path the credit was silent *by construction*, and a coordinator reference alone would have changed nothing. The replay moved to the registration, so the credit and the announcement happen together. The mutation that puts it back in `preStart` is what proves the move was necessary. **A third, one level up.** `coordinatorEvents` is a broadcast stream, so an event emitted with no listener is dropped — and an app using `LibSpiffyActorSystem` can only subscribe after `initialize()` returns, while everything reported at startup is emitted from `preStart`. Whether a startup report arrived was a race with the app's own `.listen`. Events emitted before anything listens are now kept (bounded at 256) and handed to the first listener, then the window closes — it is a startup buffer, not a replay log. **This was already true of V-112's unfinished-channel report** and would have been true of every later startup report (libspiffy-4gy8) | `test/integration/parked_receive_survives_restart_test.dart` — the bead's own test (park, restart, deliver the header, assert the event AND the credit) plus the case that had no coverage at all before this change: a header that arrives while the node is down, written straight into the header store between the two boots. A third pins the verdict rule by delivering a receive with no reply target at all — the shape a replay has — and asserting nothing is announced while it is still waiting, and that the header settling it IS announced. Two more in `channel_startup_sweep_test.dart` for the buffer, including that a second listener gets no replay. Seven mutations, each failing alone: the coordinator answer removed, the registration removed, the replay removed from the registration, the replay put back in `preStart`, the still-waiting guard dropped, the buffer disabled, and the buffer left open to every later listener. **One correction worth recording**: the first mutation run looked green at `pass 2 fail 0`, which was not a green mutation but a **segfault** — the hung test made the harness tear an Isar instance down mid-flight and the process died at exit 134, so two tests never reported. Replacing the `Stream.first.timeout` with the file's own polling helper made the same mutation fail cleanly and named. A mutation result that is not a clean red is not a result |

| V-117 | Medium | The wallet answered failures with bare `Map`s, and the coordinator did not recognise them, so a refused delete, recording, release or split left the app waiting for an answer that never came | `internal_messages.dart`, `wallet_messages.dart`, `wallet_manager_actor.dart`, `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart`, `payment_channel_aggregate.dart`, `payment_channel_messages.dart`, `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart`, `wallet_coordinator_actor.dart`, `aggregate_signing_client.dart`, `benford_coordinator_actor.dart`, `payment_coordinator_actor.dart`, `invoice_coordinator_actor.dart` | Three producers answered with untyped data: `WalletManagerActor` at three sites (`{'error', 'type'}` from its catch-all, `{'error', 'walletId'}` for a wallet with no journal and for a load failure), `BitcoinWalletAggregate`'s fallback for the roughly twenty of thirty-five commands it has no typed reply for (`{'error', 'command'}`), and `PaymentChannelAggregate`, which answered success with a **raw `List<Event>`** and failure with `{'success': false, ...}`. Eleven receivers recognised all of them the same way -- `payload is Map` -- and **the bead's central claim was checked rather than accepted**: every one of those receivers is the `sender` on `_walletManager.tell(WalletCommandMessage(...))`, which either routes to the aggregate or fails to route, so a single receiver really does see both producers and the two could not be converted separately. **The design answer is a shared base, not one type per producer.** `FailureResponse` (an `ActorResponse` whose `success` is always false and whose `error` is never null) is implemented by `WalletManagerFailure` and `WalletCommandFailed`; a receiver that only needs "this failed, and why" matches the base and keeps working when a third is added, and one that cares which actor gave up tests the concrete type -- which is precisely what `payload is Map` could not do. `PaymentChannelAggregate` answers `ChannelCommandResult`, which **carries the events** because they are load-bearing (the manager forwards each `ChannelEvent` to the P2P broadcaster and reads the lock time off the journaled `ChannelRequestedEvent`), and keeps the rule that an empty list is a success (libspiffy-y8x3); the nineteen `_askAggregate` call sites are unchanged because the funnel still returns the events. **The defect the types were hiding.** `WalletCoordinatorActor.onMessage` had no `is Map` arm, so those replies fell through to `_log.fine('Unhandled message type')`: a delete, recording, release or split the wallet refused produced **no `CoordinatorEvent` at all**, and an app that learns everything through `coordinatorEvents` waited forever. It now emits an `ErrorEvent` naming the request, or fails a still-pending creation by name. **Two more sites found while fixing it, and the bead's list was wrong about them**: `_handleRecordOutgoing` and `_handleReleaseUTXOs` told the wallet manager with **no sender at all**, so there was nobody to answer, and `_handleTransactionRecordedResponse` -- the arm that would have announced a recording -- was dead code. Both now pass `context.self`. The dead handler was **deleted rather than made live**, because it published `amountSatoshis: BigInt.zero` for an amount it did not hold; that and its live twin are filed (libspiffy-5ml6) | `wallet_coordinator_wallet_created_test.dart` gains the group that pins the defect: a command the manager cannot route, and one the aggregate refuses, each reach the app as an `ErrorEvent` naming the request; a creation the manager gives up on is failed by name rather than generically; a failure naming no wallet still arrives. All four time out on the old code. The reply shapes stay enforced by the repo's own definition of done -- `actor_reply_shape_characterization_test.dart` gained a third map, `failureReplies`, because a failure-only reply cannot honestly be built for the success path the other two maps require, and `actor_response_base_test.dart` checks it. Eleven test files were re-aimed from the map shapes onto the types. Eight mutations, each failing alone: the coordinator's arm removed, its pending-creation branch removed, the release sender removed, and each of the four producers' bare shapes restored, plus `_askAggregate` no longer checking success. **One correction worth recording**: that last mutation first came back GREEN because it was pointed at tests that ask the aggregate directly -- the leaf, not the wiring; `payment_channel_manager_rejection_test.dart` catches it, and eight tests go red there |

| V-118 | Medium | Sixteen actor replies could not be asked whether they had worked, and three encoded failure as a plausible value: a fee of zero, a status of `'error'`, and an error smuggled into the map that holds fee rates | `wallet_messages.dart`, `spv_messages.dart`, `invoice_messages.dart`, `header_sync_actor.dart`, `import_actor.dart`, `arc_actor.dart`, `spv_actor.dart` | Forty-three replies already extended `ActorResponse`; these sixteen implemented `Message` only, so a caller holding one could not ask the question every other reply answers. Conversion is source-compatible for receivers (dactor extracts `payload`, which returns `this`). **Where a success/failure pair already existed under other names it was reused rather than duplicated** (AGENTS.md, One Thing Not Two): `SPVValidationResult.isValid`/`validationError` and `InvoiceDetailsResponse.found`/`error` and `ImportCancelResponse.accepted` now also report as `success`/`error`, so nothing has two ways to say the same thing. **Two types were deleted, not converted**: `spv_messages.dart`'s `TransactionValidationResult` (no sends, no receives) and its `BEEFValidationResult`, which `spv_actor.dart` explicitly hid because the live one is in `wallet_messages.dart` -- the `hide` clause went with it. **Three replies were dishonest, not merely untyped, and that is the part worth doing**: `FeeEstimateMessage` signalled failure as `BigInt.zero`, a number an app would happily build a transaction with, and now carries a nullable fee; `FeeQuoteMessage`'s only field was a `Map` and three of its four send sites put `{'error': ...}` inside it, so `feeData['mining']` was simply absent, and it now carries the failure outside the data; `TransactionStatusMessage` reported `status: 'error'` -- a status string that is not a status, indistinguishable from one ARC really reported -- and its status is now null when the check failed. **The bead named two of those three; the status sentinel was found by reading the send sites.** `BroadcastSuccessMessage`, `BroadcastFailedMessage` and `SPVErrorMessage` have an outcome fixed by the type, so their `success` is a constant rather than a constructor argument, and they stay ordinary `ActorResponse`s: `FailureResponse` means specifically *the actor had no reply of its own*, which is not true of a named failure notification | `arc_actor_answers_without_a_number_test.dart` -- **the three failure branches had no test at all**, which is how a zero fee survived. It drives ARCActor with an ARC that throws and requires the quote to keep the error out of `feeData`, the status check to report no status, and a fourth test to require directly that a failed reply carries no value in place of the one it could not measure. The repo's definition of done did the rest: every converted type is now in `actor_reply_shape_characterization_test.dart`, whose maps are typed, so a reply that stops extending `ActorResponse` fails to compile rather than quietly losing `success`; a third map, `fixedOutcomeReplies`, holds the three whose outcome is their name. Four mutations: the quote's error back inside `feeData`, the status sentinel restored, a converted type back to `implements Message`, and a failed estimate reporting zero again -- **that last one was GREEN at first**, because nothing pinned the absence, only the `success` flag; the test that requires the value to be absent is what makes it red. **Two limits found and filed rather than papered over** (libspiffy-8743): `ARCActor.preStart` builds a TAAL mainnet service when handed none, so every `_arcService == null` branch is dead; and `_handleEstimateFee` catches a policy failure itself and falls back to an invented 1 sat/1000 bytes, so the honest failure reply this change added is unreachable there. That fallback is pinned as a characterization, labelled as one |

| V-119 | Medium | Money a reservation or a deferred payment's hold had committed was reported nowhere: it was in no balance the coordinator gave out, and a wallet frozen by a journaled hold was told only that it had no funds | `coordinator_messages.dart`, `wallet_coordinator_actor.dart`, `wallet_balances.dart`, `channel_funding.dart`, `deferred_payments.dart`, `wallet_projection.dart` | Two silences, not two wrong numbers, and both halves of the bead held. **(1)** `BalanceResponse` carried confirmed, unconfirmed, total and watch-only balances and no reserved one, and `_handleGetBalance` read `getPaymentUTXOs`, whose contract on every backend is `isAvailable && !isPluginManaged`, so a reserved row was filtered out before the handler could see it -- through the supported coordinator API the money simply vanished from every number, although the projection has written `reservedBalance` to the wallet row all along. **The decision the bead left open -- is reserved money part of `totalBalance`? -- was settled by the neighbour that already answers it**: `watchOnlyBalance` is the wallet's money, not spendable, and deliberately not part of `totalBalance`. Reserved money is the same shape, so it gets the same treatment; anything else makes two adjacent fields mean different things. **It is computed, not read from the wallet row's snapshot of it.** The brief's cheapest option was one extra `getWallet`, but mixing a live computation for three buckets with a projection snapshot for the fourth is exactly the dishonesty this seam is about -- the two could be a moment apart. One `getUTXOs` now feeds all four, which is one storage read rather than today's one plus a second. **(2)** `WalletBalances.noneSelectableReason` built its candidate list from the available UTXOs alone, so a reserved one was dropped **before any reason was computed** and the caller got the bare headline -- including the wallet frozen by a journaled hold, which is the one case the "inputs of a deferred payment" branch exists for: that branch read `legacyDeferredHeldKeys`, which a journaled hold does not enter, so it could fire only for a payment recorded before holds were journaled. The bead's extra finding held too: `ChannelFunding._noCandidatesReason` passed a `held` predicate to narrow a diagnosis the status filter had already thrown away, so for a journaled hold it was **dead code**. The predicate is deleted rather than re-aimed (One Thing, Not Two); the walk asks `WalletBalances.isDeferredHeld` itself, for every caller. **A hold and a reservation are both `reserved` and they are different answers**, so there are two branches, not one: a reservation expires and cleanup releases it, while a hold is by the payment's txid, has no expiry, and ends only when the network settles the payment, ARC reports it failed, or the user cancels or reclaims it. The mark that tells them apart, `reservationReason == 'deferred-spend'`, was a string written in three places; it is now `WalletBalances.deferredHoldReason`, which `DeferredPayments.holdReason` and the projection reference. **Scope beyond the bead**: the coordinator's new loop restated the bucket rule `WalletBalances.bucketOf` already owns, and so did the projection's; both now ask it, so the three layers cannot drift. Also removed a dead `network_name.dart` import in `channel_funding.dart` | `test/core/wallet/reserved_funds_are_reported_test.dart` -- twelve tests over both halves. The balance half drives the real coordinator over in-memory storage: the bead's own case (a wallet whose only funds are a stuck funding's held inputs reports them as reserved and its total as zero), a mixed wallet pinning that reserved money is apart from the spendable balance **and that a pending UTXO still counts nowhere** (the guard on the source switch), and that an unreserved wallet reports zero rather than null. The reason half builds the hold through the real `TransactionSpendDeferredEvent` and `applySpendDeferred` rather than setting a `reservationReason` by hand, and pins that channel funding and the Benford split say the same thing about the same wallet (bead f4qy's rule), that an ordinary reservation is named as a payment in flight and not as a hold, that a legacy inferred hold is still named, and that a wallet holding nothing at all still gets the bare headline. **The ordering tests were wrong first and the code was right**: a wallet holding one token and one reserved output is told about the reservation, because the walk *narrows* -- it names the exclusion that emptied the wallet, not the first kind of output it saw. Precedence only shows on one output carrying two exclusions at once, which is how they are written now, and a journaled hold is exactly such an output: it is a reservation too, and the more specific answer wins. Five mutations, each failing alone with the expected test names: the field not sent, `getPaymentUTXOs` restored as the source, the walk back to available-only, `isDeferredHeld` back to legacy holds only, and the reserved branch moved ahead of the hold branch |

| V-120 | Medium | An app that recorded its own payment was told it had **received** that payment, incoming, for zero satoshis — once per change output — and was told nothing about the recording | `coordinator_messages.dart`, `wallet_coordinator_actor.dart`, `wallet_messages.dart`, `bitcoin_wallet_aggregate.dart`, `spv-understanding.md` | The bead named two defects. Both held, and **the first was wrong in the safer direction**: it read as a cosmetic `BigInt.zero` on an event nobody could see, and it is a live lie about which way the money went. `_handleUTXOReceivedResponse` emitted `TransactionReceivedEvent(amountSatoshis: BigInt.zero, isIncoming: true)`, and **V-117 turned that arm on**: it added `sender: context.self` to `_handleRecordOutgoing` so a refused recording would reach the app, and the aggregate answers a recording with one `UTXOReceivedResponse` per output that pays the wallet itself (`_addWalletOutputs` credits change, settlement and self-transfer outputs). **Verified by running it before changing anything**: a coordinator-level `RecordOutgoingCommand` for a payment with one change output produces exactly one app-facing event, `TransactionReceivedEvent(txid: <the payment>, amount: 0, isIncoming: true)`. Three wrong things in one event — direction, amount, and the recording never announced. **The bead offered two designs for it (carry the amount, or make the field nullable) and both are wrong, because the premise is**: the receive this event claims to report is already reported, honestly, by the events the standalone receive path emits — `SPVValidationResultEvent` with the UTXOs and `TransactionImportedEvent` with `utxosCreated` and `totalValueReceived`, emitted only after the projection has applied it. A fourth announcement of the same thing is One Thing, Not Two; change from your own payment is not an incoming receive at all. So `TransactionReceivedEvent` and its handler are **deleted**, not fixed. **A third finding, in the documentation**: `spv-understanding.md`'s receive flow listed step 9 as "Coordinator emits TransactionReceivedEvent on public stream", a step that never existed — the wallet manager issues `ReceiveUTXOCommand` with no sender, so nothing on that path could ever answer the coordinator. Corrected to the events the coordinator actually emits. **The recording is now announced**, by a new `TransactionRecordedEvent`. Its amount is read off the `TransactionRecordedEvent` the command journaled, not off the command, so it is what the wallet recorded rather than what the caller asked for — and it is **null, not zero, when nothing was journaled** because the wallet held the transaction already (bead libspiffy-viy) and there is no event to read it off. It waits for the projection before announcing, the promise `WalletCreatedEvent` (V-p56) and `TransactionImportedEvent` already make and the one the deleted handler did not: an app told its payment is recorded queries for it next | `test/actors/outgoing_recording_is_announced_test.dart` — six tests over a real `WalletManagerActor` and a real wallet aggregate, so the change-output replies genuinely happen, with a stub projection that holds the awaiter on cue rather than racing it. The bead's own test (recorded, with the amount it recorded); **the lie, pinned as a count**: a payment with two of the wallet's own outputs is announced ONCE, where the old code announced it twice and as a receive; the absence (recording the same transaction again is announced with no amount rather than a zero); V-117's property kept (a recording the manager cannot route still arrives as an `ErrorEvent`); and two for the projection barrier, including a projection that never applies it being reported as recorded-but-not-applied rather than as a plain success. Five mutations, each red alone and each against the right tests: the receive lie restored, the recording arm removed, the amount taken from the command instead of the journaled event, a zero in place of the absence, and the projection wait dropped |

| V-121 | Medium | ARC invented a fee rate when the policy could not be read, and invented a **mainnet** endpoint when given none — so a wallet with no ARC configured silently talked to TAAL mainnet, and seven honest "ARC service not available" branches were dead | `arc_actor.dart`, `wallet_messages.dart`, `wallet_coordinator_actor.dart` | **The fix was already argued for in the same file, by the sibling method.** `_quotePolicyFee` (bead libspiffy-87a) carries the doc comment "Unlike `_handleEstimateFee` it does not fall back to a guessed rate: a policy ARC could not be asked for is answered as a failure, so a caller never builds a transaction at a fee nobody quoted" — so the codebase held the rule and applied it to one of two siblings. `_handleEstimateFee` wrapped the policy fetch in its own try/catch and fell back to `const ArcFeeAmount(satoshis: 1, bytes: 1000)`, reporting **success** with a rate nothing published; V-118's honest failure reply was unreachable behind it. It now answers as its sibling does. **(2) The silent mainnet default is the last copy of the defect audit V-2 fixed.** `_initializeARCService` built `ArcServiceConfig.taalMainnet()` whenever it was handed no config — `LibSpiffyActorSystem` resolves the endpoint from the wallet's network (V-2) and so never passes null, but a direct construction did, and a testnet wallet would have been broadcasting to mainnet. **Chosen deliberately between the bead's two options: no configuration now means no ARC, rather than deleting the dead guards.** A wallet with no ARC is a reasonable configuration — it holds, records and proves transactions and asks nobody to broadcast them — and every handler already answers "ARC service not available" for it. So the change brings **seven existing branches to life** instead of removing them, and takes out a wrong default. **A third finding, same defect class, same file**: `PolicyFeeQuote.fee` was documented as "zero when success is false" — the very shape V-118 took out of `FeeEstimateMessage`. Its one caller guarded (`!quote.success \|\| fee <= BigInt.zero`), but the type is public and the next caller would not. It is nullable now, and the guard reads `fee == null` beside its other checks | `arc_actor_answers_without_a_number_test.dart` — **this bead's two defects were pinned in that file as characterizations by V-118, labelled as such, and this change flips them**, which is the cheapest possible regression test: the fee estimate that reported success with an invented rate now reports the failure and no fee. A new group covers the newly supported state, an ARCActor with neither config nor service: a broadcast refused rather than sent to a mainnet endpoint nobody asked for, a fee estimate with no fee, a fee quote with no rates, a status check with no status, a merkle proof request refused — five branches that could not be reached before. Plus a policy quote carrying no fee when it failed. Four mutations, each red alone: the invented rate restored, the silent mainnet default restored, the estimate's no-ARC guard dropped, and a zero back on the failed policy quote. **One existing test needed its assertion changed and it was the right change, not a workaround**: `arc_actor_deferred_payment_test.dart`'s "a policy ARC cannot be asked for is a failure, not a guessed rate" asserted `fee == BigInt.zero`; the property in its own name is unchanged and null states it more strongly |

| V-122 | Low | Coordinator results and internal actor messages still handed out the collections they were built from, and every subscriber of the coordinator's broadcast stream shared one instance of each result | `coordinator_messages.dart`, `wallet_messages.dart`, `spv_messages.dart`, `invoice_messages.dart`, `payment_messages.dart` | The two directions V-116 left open, **71 fields** (the bead counted 77: `spv_messages.dart` has 10, not 16). **The decision the bead required be made deliberately -- freezing a result is a public-API behaviour change, an app that sorts one in place now throws `UnsupportedError` -- was settled by a fact, not a preference.** The objection is "a result is the app's own copy". It is not: `WalletCoordinatorActor.events` is a `StreamController.broadcast`, so every subscriber is handed the **same instance**, and one app listener sorting its result reorders it for every other listener. That is exactly the sharing V-116 argued from for the inbound direction (the projection, subscribers and broadcaster reading one event), one layer further out. **Same evidence standard as V-116 before freezing**: `lib/` was searched for in-place mutation of every one of the 71 and none was found (the four textual hits are a private `_PendingProof` set in `arc_actor.dart` and dartsv's own `Transaction.outputs`). 60 fields went through the V-116 script; 11 had shapes it does not handle and were done by hand -- five positional constructors, and six spv messages whose `_metadata = metadata ?? {}` kept the caller's map behind the `metadata` getter every `Message` exposes | `frozen_collections_guard_test.dart` extended rather than duplicated: it scans the whole of `coordinator_messages.dart` and the four internal files, and expects **139** fields. Three behavioural tests in `aggregate_state_immutability_test.dart`'s new `a0fk` group, the first of them pinning the premise that decided the question: a **real coordinator** with two subscribers, where both receive an `identical` result, neither can sort it, and the other still reads it in order. Then the outbound events and the internal messages each copying and freezing what they are built from, nested maps included. Seven mutations, each red alone with the expected test names. **One worth recording**: unfreezing only `UTXOSplitCompleteEvent.txids` leaves the end-to-end test GREEN, because the `SplitUTXOsResponse` it is built from is frozen too -- the direct type test catches it, and only the real pre-fix wiring (both unfrozen) reddens the end-to-end one. A field no behavioural test touches (`TransactionConfirmationsRevertedMessage.txids`) is caught by the guard alone. **The full suite surfaced one failure, and it was not this change**: `imported_transaction_proof_confirms_test.dart` waited for P's output row and then asserted P's transaction row existed at that same instant. The two are written from different journal events (`UTXOReceived`, `TransactionImported`) that nothing orders for an observer, so under full-suite load the second could lag — a null check, not the `UnsupportedError` freezing produces, and the file is untouched since V-63. It now waits for both rows |
| V-123 | High | A submission ARC answered `REJECTED` was replied to as a successful broadcast, so a channel whose funding the network refused marked the funding inputs spent | `arc_actor.dart`, `wallet_messages.dart`, `arc_service.dart` | ARC answers a submission with an HTTP 200 whatever status the transaction reached, `REJECTED` and `DOUBLE_SPEND_ATTEMPTED` included, and `_handleBroadcastTransaction` / `_handleBroadcastBEEF` replied `BroadcastSuccessMessage(txid, networkTxid)` to every 200 -- a reply with **no status in it**, so no caller could have told the difference even had it tried. Three callers act on that reply: `PaymentChannelManagerActor` took a rejected **funding** as broadcast and ran `_spendFundingInputs` (the wallet's inputs marked spent for a transaction the network refused -- money gone from the balance), a rejected **refund** as claimed, and `SettleBEEFCommand` counted a rejected child as submitted. Found while tracing what a received payment's submission reports (the receive seam, next rows): the bead asked about a scan, the reply was the defect. Fixed at the one place every caller reads: `_submitReply` turns a definitive failure (`DeferredNetworkStatus.isDefinitiveFailure`, the rule deferred payments already use) into `BroadcastFailedMessage(networkStatus: 'REJECTED')` carrying ARC's reason, and every other answer into `BroadcastSuccessMessage(networkStatus:)` -- `DOUBLE_SPEND_ATTEMPTED` stays a success, because ARC holds the transaction and either spend may still be mined (bead libspiffy-ey2), and the caller is now told which it is. **Two more lies in the same reply, fixed with it**: a submission that threw was queued for durable retry and the caller told only "failed" -- `BroadcastFailedMessage.willRetry` now says so (what a channel should do with a failure that will be retried is bead libspiffy-r56l); and `ArcSubmitResponse` read ARC's reason from `message` only while ARC's `TransactionResponse` carries it in `extraInfo` (the status parser read both), so a rejection's reason was always lost -- both parsers now read it through one `_arcReason`. **Leave it better**: `ArcTransactionStatus` was mapped to ARC's wire names three times (two identical 55-line switches in the parsers, the inverse switch `ARCActor.arcWireStatus`); it is now an enhanced enum with `wireName` and `fromWire`, and `arcWireStatus` is deleted | `test/actors/arc_submit_answer_is_reported_test.dart` (13 tests, both submit messages): mutation R1 (`_submitReply` always a success, the old behaviour) turns both `REJECTED` tests red -- `Expected: <Instance of 'BroadcastFailedMessage'> Actual: <Instance of 'BroadcastSuccessMessage'>`; R2 (retry not reported) turns the queued-retry test red; R3 (submit parser reads `message` only) turns both `REJECTED` tests red on the missing reason. The status parse itself is pinned by `arc_service_test.dart`, green over the enum. The full suite turned up a second instance of the race V-122 fixed in `imported_transaction_proof_confirms_test.dart` (a failure of a class this change cannot produce: `pending` read off a row whose output had just turned `available`): 73bj (1) now waits for the output and the transaction row together, since the projection writes them separately |
| V-124 | High | A counterparty's payment was submitted to ARC on one path only, as a fire-and-forget tell announced `broadcasted: true` before ARC answered; a payment that waited for a header was never submitted; `ReceiveTransactionCommand` and `ImportTransactionCommand` recorded unproven payments that nothing submitted | `wallet_coordinator_actor.dart`, `coordinator_messages.dart`, `spv_actor.dart`, `wallet_messages.dart`, `beef.dart`, `spv-understanding.md` | **The model, as the owner stated it on 21 Sep 2026**: the counterparty hands us the payment with its ancestors' proofs; we check those against our headers (anchoring, not double-spend protection); and **we** submit the payment to ARC, because we are the party that cares about being paid -- from then on it is our broadcast, tracked to acceptance and to its block, with our outputs' proof from ARC. This seam started from bead onh and the question of whether the status scan may ask ARC about pending incoming rows; it may, *because* we broadcast them -- and tracing that showed which receives were never broadcast. Four defects on one path: (1) only `ValidateBEEFCommand` submitted, by `_arcActor.tell(BroadcastBEEFMessage)` with no sender, and emitted `BEEFValidationResultEvent(broadcasted: true)` in the same breath -- before ARC answered, whatever it answered (V-123: a `REJECTED`), and with no ARC configured at all; overnode relays that flag to the payer. (2) The submission raced the projection: ARC's status update is applied to the read-model row, which the recording might not have written yet (`_handleTransactionStatusUpdated` returns when there is no row). (3) A payment whose ancestor's proof named a header we had not synced was parked by SPVActor, whose first reply is "waiting", not a verdict. That reply consumed the txid-FIFO correlation, so the verdict after the header arrived was announced as an import and **never submitted**; after a restart the replay has no caller at all. (4) `ReceiveTransactionCommand` -- the receive path `spv-understanding.md` documented -- and `ImportTransactionCommand` were the same pipeline and submitted nothing, so a payment received that way was polled on ARC by the status scan though nobody had given it to ARC. **Fix, one rule for every way a verdict arrives**: every receive the coordinator asks for carries a requestId and is recorded as a payment or an import (`_receives`, replacing the txid FIFO); a verdict nobody is correlated to -- a parked receive replayed, in-process or after a restart -- is a payment if it names an invoice or its subject carried no proof. `SPVValidationResult` gains `invoiceId` (echoed, and stored with a parked receive already), `awaitingHeader` (not a verdict) and `subjectCarriesProof` (`BEEF.carriesProofOf`, new). A payment is answered off the mailbox by `_answerPayment`: it waits for the read model to hold the payment, submits it from the stored row with an **ask** (`BroadcastTransactionMessage`, so V-123's honest reply comes back), and emits `BEEFValidationResultEvent` with `broadcasted` (ARC accepted), `networkStatus`, `broadcastError`, `awaitingHeader`. A payment whose own proof verified is already mined and is submitted nowhere. **Owner decisions (21 Sep 2026)**: `ImportTransactionCommand` is for recovery and our own history, so it accepts only a BEEF carrying its subject's proof (refused otherwise, naming `ValidateBEEFCommand`), and takes its txid from the BEEF -- the caller's `transactionId` is removed (overnode invented `import-<ms>` when it had none, and SPVActor carried that as the verdict's txid); `ReceiveTransactionCommand` is **deleted** (One Thing, Not Two). overnode_v2 receives payments through `ImportTransactionCommand` and must move to `ValidateBEEFCommand`: bead libspiffy-3ps5, not edited from here | `test/integration/received_payment_is_submitted_test.dart` (7 tests, regtest, real-PoW headers, an ARC that records submissions): X1 (the old fire-and-forget: tell, then `broadcasted` at once) turns the REJECTED test and the waits-for-ARC test red; X2 (an uncorrelated verdict is an import, the old routing) turns the header-wait test red -- P never reaches ARC; X3 (no Import gate) turns the refused-import test red; X4 (no wait for the read model) turns 4 red; X5 (a payment proven mined is submitted anyway) turns the already-mined test red. Nine receive tests moved off `ReceiveTransactionCommand`, onto the command their BEEF calls for (`test/integration/receive_helpers.dart` picks by `carriesProofOf`, so the choice is the BEEF's, not the test's); `beef_retained_until_header_test` now also asserts the first answer is `awaitingHeader`. Two `wallet_coordinator_correlation_test` results built by hand now say they are imports (`subjectCarriesProof: true`). **Found by the rp6x test on the way**: the payment answer carried no `unreadableOutputs` -- outputs the wallet could not read, and so did not credit -- though the import answer always had, and the invoice path is now the one way in; `BEEFValidationResultEvent.unreadableOutputs` added (frozen-collection guard **140**), pinned by a new rp6x test (mutation: field not passed, `Expected: [{vout: 3, ...}] Actual: []`). **Found by the full suite**: six integration fakes of ARC answered no submission, so each now-submitted payment went over HTTP to `fake://arc`, failed, and was queued for retry in Isar -- and `parked_receive_survives_restart_test` then closed Isar with that work in flight and the process died with SEGV in `libisar`. The fakes now answer `STORED` (ARC holds it; nothing is settled by it); the product hazard, that `LibSpiffyActorSystem.shutdown` returns while ARCActor may still write its Isar retry queue, is bead libspiffy-vr89 -- not fixed by skipping the enqueue after stop, which would lose a durable retry. The three 4gy8 tests in `parked_receive_survives_restart_test.dart` (a replayed receive reaches the app, with the amount) now expect what a replayed **payment** is answered with, `BEEFValidationResultEvent`, and read the amount off its `spendableUTXOs`: the property is unchanged, the event is the payment's own |
| V-125 | Low | When ARC reported a submission on the network before the read model held its recording, the change output became available only at the next status scan, which asked ARC again for an answer it had given | `arc_actor.dart` | Bead onh, from 09k (V-16). The deferred spend is driven by what the read model still shows outstanding, which is right -- it makes the spend idempotent across a submit answer, scans and MINED reports -- but it means an answer that arrives before the projection has written the recording finds no change output to promote. The inputs are spent at once (their reservation rows were projected long before); the change waited up to `statusCheckInterval` (30 s by default), and the scan that finally promoted it spent an ARC query on a status already known. **The bead proposed a debounced status scan a second later; that would ask ARC again**, and it is not needed: ARC's answer is in hand, only the read model is late. So `_applyDeferredSpend` now notes when the transaction's own row is missing and applies the spend again **from storage** after `deferredSpendRecheckDelay` (1 s), until the row appears -- the recording's `UTXOReceivedEvent`s are journaled before its `TransactionRecordedEvent`, so the row being there says the outputs are -- or until a periodic scan would have covered it anyway (bounded by `statusCheckInterval` from the report; `postStop` cancels pending rechecks). No ARC call is added anywhere. The received payments of V-124 never hit this (they are submitted after the read model holds them); our own broadcasts -- payments, channel funding, splits, reclaims -- can | Three tests in `arc_actor_submit_deferred_spend_test.dart`'s new `onh` group (SEEN_ON_NETWORK and MINED: only the input's reservation projected when ARC answers, the recording projected afterwards, scan interval 10 minutes; the change is promoted within seconds, `getTransactionCalls == 0`, the input is spent once), plus a bound: a recording the read model never shows is looked up about once a second and then no more, counted by a storage wrapper. Mutation O1 (no recheck) turns both promotion tests red, `Expected: [...:1] Actual: []`; O2 (no bound) turns the bound test red |

Now each funding UTXO also comes as a `PluginFundingInput`: the outpoint over its **real** locking script and a *factory* of the unlocking script the wallet writes for it (factories because `buildWithSigner` re-runs the build per signing pass and a builder keeps the signatures each pass adds). The choice of unlocking script is `WalletTransactionSigner.unlockingScriptFor`, the script half of `unlockFor`, which `unlockFor` now also builds its unlockers with, so the wallet and a plugin write the same unlocking script for the same output; the P2PKH shape test is one function, `isP2pkhScript`, shared with `TransactionSize`. **Opt-in** (`TransactionBuilderPlugin.spendsAnyWalletOutput`, default false): only a plugin that says it spends through `fundingInputs` is funded from non-P2PKH outputs, for payments and `ProvisionFundingMessage` alike, so an existing plugin (overnode_v2's tstoken plugin, the only one in the workspace) keeps working unchanged. **The signer could not sign m-of-n, m >= 2**, as the bead's own survey found: dartsv calls a signer once per input, and `_AggregateBackedSigner` added one signature cached under `sighashType:digest`, so a 2-of-2 input got one signature (and a second call would have got the same one). It now adds all m, each cached under its own index and signed by the aggregate with the i-th of the script's keys the wallet holds, in script order - the order `OP_CHECKMULTISIG` reads them, and the keys the aggregate's own `_multisigSigningKeys` picks; a single-signature input is resolved exactly as before. **The fee rate**: the request carries `feeRate`, ARC's policy rate the payment asked for (V-132), so a plugin's transactions can pay what every other transaction pays; the provisioning message path now asks ARC for it too. **A latent bug the opt-in exposed**: the earmark UTXOs auto-provisioning hands the plugin were recorded with `scriptPubKey: sourceUtxo.scriptPubKey`, which is their real script only when the source is P2PKH; with a multisig source the plugin would have signed each earmark over the multisig script. They carry the P2PKH script they pay; and the provisioning split spends its source with the source's own unlocking script | `coordinator_aggregate_signing_test.dart` +3, through the whole system with the wallet signing for real and every input run through `Interpreter.correctlySpends` against its own locking script: an opted-in plugin paid from a 2-of-2 multisig UTXO and a P2PK UTXO (the multisig input carries `OP_0` and two signatures; the request carries signature counts 2 and 1 and ARC's rate); a plugin needing two funding UTXOs auto-provisioned from a 2-of-2 multisig source (split, two earmarks, payment - all four transactions valid); `ProvisionFundingMessage` from a 2-of-2 multisig UTXO. The three existing tests pinning P2PKH-only funding for a plugin that does not opt in are unchanged and green. Nine mutations, each red alone: the payment ignoring the opt-in (2 red), one signature per input (3), the digest-only cache key (3), the first key signing twice (3), the locking script rebuilt as P2PKH (3), the provision ignoring the opt-in (1), an invented rate in the request (1), earmarks recorded with the source's script (1), the provisioning split unlocking its source as P2PKH (1). Test helpers `fund`/`fundWithImportedParent` take a locking script. **Downstream**: overnode_v2's plugin test constructs `PluginTransactionRequest` and must pass `fundingInputs` and `feeRate` (noted on bead 3ps5); its plugin can opt in once it spends through `fundingInputs` |
| V-137 | Low | `ArcPolicyResponse` offered ARC's one published rate under three names | `arc_service.dart` | The last copy, found by the fees seam's closing sweep of `lib/` for rates, of what V-132 removed from ARCActor's replies ("mining" and "relay" copies of the same number): `standardFeePerKb`, `minFeePerKb` and `dataFeePerKb` all returned `miningFee.satoshisPerKb`, their doc comments conceding that ARC publishes no separate relay or data fee. Three names for one value invite a caller to believe they can differ. Removed; `miningFee` (a `FeeRate`) is the rate. Nothing in `lib/` or in overnode_v2 read them | A removal, not a behaviour: the two tests that read the getters (`arc_service_test.dart`) read `miningFee.satoshisPerKb` instead; `dart analyze` over `lib/` and `test/` is clean and the full suite green. | V-138 | High | A channel acted on messages from any peer: a third party could end, close or take over someone else's channel | `channel_p2p_adapter.dart` | Found by the adversarial review of the channel protocol (22 Sep, finding F3). `ChannelP2PAdapter.handleP2PMessage` dispatched on the channel id in the payload and never compared `fromPeerId` with the channel's counterparty. Channel ids are UUIDs but not secrets: both parties hold them and every relay carries them. A third peer could send `channel_closed` or `channel_reject` (the client dropped its records and told the app its channel had ended), `channel_close` (our side journaled a close), `payment_ack` / `refund_signed` (answers in the server's name), or `channel_accept` before the real server did — overwriting `_channelPeers`, so the client funded a 2-of-2 with the intruder's key and sent its refund to the intruder to sign. On the server, a second `channel_request` with the same id replaced the pending request, so the app's acceptance applied to the intruder's keys. **Fix:** every message but a new request names a channel that must be on record, and must come from the counterparty `_counterpartyPeer` already derived for outbound messages, in the role that sends it (server-only: accept, reject, refund_signed, payment_ack; client-only: refund_sign_request, channel_open, payment_update). A request for a channel already pending or held with another peer is refused. Refusals are logged and not answered. The client's record now takes both peers from its journaled `ChannelRequestedEvent`, as a restored record did; the test that modelled a channel with no known server now journals none instead of one it then ignored (libspiffy-i0e6). | `test/actors/channel_counterparty_test.dart` (8 tests, both roles, with positive controls). Four mutations on the fix: dispatching from any peer (6 red), no role check (1 red: a server-sent `payment_update` acted on by the client), every request treated as new (1 red: the intruder's key accepted), peers not taken from the journal (1 red: the real server refused). A fifth — the server record also setting peers from its event — survived because the server record already names its client, and the line was removed |
| V-139 | High | The channel server acknowledged a payment whose client signature did not verify, and held nothing it could broadcast | `payment_channel_aggregate.dart` | Found by the adversarial review of the channel protocol (22 Sep, finding F5). A payment is paid only when the client's half of the 2-of-2 signature signs the transaction the server holds. The manager has the server's wallet sign, then `_fullySignedPayment` combines both halves and verifies the result against the funding output — and on failure logged a warning and returned `''`, and `AcknowledgePaymentCommand` journaled the acknowledgement with an empty fully signed payment. Nothing in the aggregate looked at the client signature. A client could sign some other transaction, send it with a real payment, and have the payment acknowledged — the app's cue to deliver — while the server held no spend of the funding output. V-133 checked what the transaction pays; this is whether the client signed it at all. **Fix:** `_handleAcknowledgePayment` refuses unless the client signature verifies over the payment transaction with the channel's 2-of-2 script and funding amount. The refund already had this check inline (`_handleRecordRefundBuilt`); both now use one `_checkClientSignature` (libspiffy-c5zw). Tests that acknowledged payments with a placeholder signature (`'30' * 36`) now sign for real with a new `channelClientSignature` fixture. | `test/actors/commitment_countersign_test.dart` (3 new: signed over another transaction, signed by the server's key, not a signature). Mutations: dropping the check from the acknowledgement (3 red), letting an invalid signature pass (2 red; a malformed one still throws), and dropping it from the refund (1 red, the existing b83 guard test) |
| V-140 | Critical | Nothing broadcast a channel's settlement: a closed channel's payments went back to the client at the lock time | `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart`, `channel_p2p_adapter.dart`, `payment_channel_messages.dart`, `channel_commands.dart`, `channel_events.dart`, `payment_channel_aggregate.dart`, `wallet_coordinator_actor.dart` | Found by the adversarial review of the channel protocol (22 Sep, finding F1). The server's money in a channel exists only once its settlement — the latest payment, fully signed — is on the network before the client's refund becomes valid at the lock time; first seen wins and nothing replaces it. V-86 made a cooperative close record the settlement in the wallet as a pending receive and journal `ChannelClosedEvent` ("settlement broadcast", per `ChannelStatus.closed`), and stated "no ARC (we did not broadcast it)". Nothing else did either: no path in `lib/` submitted a settlement. A closed channel therefore still had an unspent funding output, and the client's refund took it — every payment reversed, with the server's wallet and channel read model saying it had been paid. A server's expiry recorded its settlement the same way. **Fix:** `_finalizeClose` has the server submit its settlement to ARC (`_broadcastSettlement`, `retryOnFailure: false` because the channel owns the retry, as for funding and refund, V-130) before the wallet write and `FinalizeCloseCommand`; a failure, or `DOUBLE_SPEND_ATTEMPTED` (another spend may have got there first), throws and the channel stays `closing`, the resumable middle, so closing again retries. The server's expiry broadcasts before recording, and considers only its settlement, not the client's refund. **The client's half:** `channel_closed` carried only a txid, and the client's adapter dropped its records and emitted `ChannelClosedEvent` to the host without journaling anything — the client's channel stayed open and its share never reached its wallet. The server now hands over the settlement itself (`ChannelClosedEvent.settlementTxHex`, journaled, null in older rows), and the client's new `RecordSettlementMessage` checks it is its latest payment (same outpoint, sequence, lock time, version and outputs as the template it signed last) with both signatures verifying against the funding output, journals the close if `channel_close` had not arrived, records the return leg and finalises. The client never closes with a copy of its own: its countersigned copy (bead z2px) is not one anybody broadcast. Only the server sends `channel_closed`. Close and expiry are now told with a reply target and a failure reaches the host as an `ErrorEvent`; it used to reach only the log (libspiffy-u6q6). | `test/actors/payment_channel_manager_settlement_test.dart` (3 new server close tests: broadcast before the close, a failed broadcast leaves `closing` and closing again closes, a contested one does not close; 2 server expiry tests; 5 client hand-over tests: closes and records, idempotent after `closing`, not-the-latest refused, unsigned refused, a server is not handed one; the z2px close test now asserts the client does not close with its own copy); `test/actors/channel_settlement_handover_test.dart` (5 adapter tests). Ten mutations, all red: no broadcast on close (3), contested accepted (1), client closes with its own copy (1), any settlement accepted (1), unsigned accepted (1), no broadcast at expiry (2), the old `channel_closed` handling (1), no settlement in `channel_closed` (1), close told without a reply target (1), event without the settlement (1) |
| V-141 | Critical | The channel server returned its signature on every payment: the client could broadcast an earlier state paying the server less | `channel_p2p_adapter.dart`, `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart`, `payment_channel_messages.dart`, `channel_commands.dart`, `payment_channel_aggregate.dart`, `channel_events.dart` | Found by the adversarial review of the channel protocol (22 Sep, finding F2). Every payment is a transaction spending the same 2-of-2 funding output with lock time 0. In a one-way channel only the payee may hold full signatures: whoever holds a fully signed spend of an earlier state can broadcast it, and on BSV the first spend seen wins and none replaces it. Bead z2px (V-86's follow-up) had the server send its half of each payment's signature in `payment_ack`, and the client combined and journaled it (`PaymentCountersignedEvent`) so a client close had something to record. The client therefore held a broadcastable spend of every state: having paid the server 60,000 sats, it could broadcast the state paying 30,000, and the server's later settlement would be a double spend. The probe built exactly that transaction from the server's answer and verified it against the funding output. **Fix:** `payment_ack` carries the sequence alone and `PaymentAcknowledgedResponse` no longer carries the server's signature. The client needs none: since V-140 it closes with the settlement the server broadcast and handed over. `RecordPaymentCountersignatureMessage`, `RecordPaymentCountersignatureCommand`, their handlers and the adapter's forwarding are deleted. `PaymentCountersignedEvent` is `@Deprecated` and kept, registered and applied, so client journals that hold it replay (Data Retention); the client never closes with that copy (V-140), and a settlement handed over is still matched to its latest payment. A `payment_ack` from an older server that still carries a signature is not acted on (libspiffy-pkg5). | `test/actors/channel_settlement_handover_test.dart` (the server's `payment_ack` carries neither signature nor settlement), `test/actors/channel_p2p_adapter_test.dart` (a `payment_ack` carrying a signature is not acted on; replaces the two z2px tests), `test/actors/payment_channel_manager_settlement_test.dart` (a journal holding a countersigned copy replays, does not close with it, and still accepts the settlement handed over; replaces the z2px group). Mutations: the signature back in `payment_ack` (1 red), the settlement in it (1 red), the client closing with the replayed copy (1 red). The deleted forwarding cannot be mutated back without the deleted types |
| V-142 | High | The channel server opened a channel on a funding transaction the network might never see | `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart` | Found by the adversarial review of the channel protocol (22 Sep, finding F6). On `channel_open` the server SPV-validates the funding BEEF (bead fsy: ancestors proven, scripts, no negative fee) and the aggregate checks the funding output. Nothing asked whether the network had the transaction. SPV proves ancestry, not inclusion or acceptance: a client could send the BEEF of a funding it never broadcast, or double-spend its inputs once the channel was open — first seen wins — and the server would acknowledge payments, and the app deliver, against an output that never exists. The owner's model (V-124) is that the receiver submits what it receives. **Fix:** the server submits the funding transaction to ARC after SPV validation and before journaling the open, and opens only when ARC holds it uncontested; a refusal or `DOUBLE_SPEND_ATTEMPTED` refuses the open, which the client is told of as for any refused open, and a re-sent `channel_open` (V-100) submits it again (`retryOnFailure: false`, the channel owning the retry as in V-130). The client broadcasts first, so ARC already holds it and answers with its status. The submission shares `_submitUncontested` with the settlement broadcast (V-140), so the two cannot disagree on what counts as held (libspiffy-3nje). The open path's tests had a fake ARC that answered only the policy rate; they now use one that answers broadcasts, and the e2e test that pinned `bob.arc.submitted` empty now pins it to the funding. | `test/actors/payment_channel_manager_rejection_test.dart` (3 new: submitted before the open is journaled, a refused funding does not open, a contested one does not open). Mutations: no submission (3 red), contested accepted (2 red, with the settlement's), refusal accepted (3 red, with the settlement's close and expiry) |
| V-143 | Low | The channel server accepted a channel of any funding amount, including zero and negative ones | `payment_channel_aggregate.dart` | Found by the adversarial review of the channel protocol (22 Sep). `_handleRequestChannel` refuses a funding amount that is not positive, but the amount reaches the server from the client's `channel_request`, and `_handleAcceptChannel` journaled any. No money could move — a payment's balances would go negative and be refused — but the server journaled, and showed its app, a channel that cannot exist, and one side of the protocol enforced a rule the other did not. `_handleAcceptChannel` now applies the same rule. | `test/core/payment_channel_aggregate_guards_test.dart` (2 new: 0 and -1000 refused, nothing journaled). Removing the guard turns both red |
| V-144 | High | Nothing settled a channel before its refund became valid, and a server accepted any lock time | `channel_timing.dart` (new, exported), `payment_channel_aggregate.dart`, `channel_commands.dart`, `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart`, `channel_p2p_adapter.dart`, `wallet_coordinator_actor.dart`, `libspiffy_actor_system.dart`, `README.md` | Found by the adversarial review of the channel protocol (22 Sep, finding F4). The server's payments exist only if its settlement is on the network before the client's refund becomes valid at the lock time; BSV has no replacement and the first spend seen wins. But the server acknowledged payments until `now >= lockTime` (`ChannelState.isExpired`), accepted any lock time a `channel_request` named — minutes away, or below 500,000,000 where nLockTime is a block height — and nothing settled a channel unless the app remembered to close it. V-140 made a close broadcast the settlement; this is WHEN. **Owner decision (22 Sep): the suggested design** — a settlement margin and a minimum lifetime, required and with no defaults, and the library settling by itself. **Fix:** `ChannelTiming{settlementMargin, minimumLifetime}` (validated: margin positive, lifetime longer than the margin). `initialize(channelTiming:)` hands it to the manager (a required, nullable `timing`) and to the coordinator's adapter; a node given none requests, accepts and pays no channels and says so (`No channel timing is configured`), while existing channels can still be closed, expired and refunded. The aggregate holds the four commands to it, as it holds payments to the policy fee rate (V-134): a request or acceptance with less than the minimum lifetime to run is refused, an acceptance whose lock time is a block height is refused, and no payment is recorded or acknowledged from `lockTime - margin` on. The server's adapter arms a timer per served channel when it opens, firing the ordinary close (so the settlement is broadcast first, V-140, and a failure reaches the host, V-140) when the margin begins; the coordinator re-arms them at startup from the read model, settling at once a served channel already inside its margin or left `closing`. A settlement that fails leaves the channel `closing`; closing again, or a restart, retries it. Tests that requested exactly the one-hour test minimum became flaky at a second boundary — the server measures the remaining time when it accepts — and now request two hours; the README says to request comfortably above the server's minimum. Two expiry tests now run a short timing, and the refund-claim e2e halts the server, which would otherwise settle first. **cc8m, by owner decision not a defect:** a settlement's fee is fixed when its payment is acknowledged, so a policy rise before settlement can leave it underpaying; fee changes are announced well in advance, and the README's rewritten channel section documents the risk (libspiffy-ywbk, libspiffy-cc8m). | `test/core/payment_channel_aggregate_guards_test.dart` (7: acceptance below the minimum lifetime, a block-height lock time, at the minimum accepted; a request below it; no acknowledgement and no payment inside the margin, an acknowledgement before it); `test/actors/payment_channel_manager_rejection_test.dart` (2: a node with no timing requests and accepts nothing); `test/actors/channel_settlement_handover_test.dart` (4: settled when the margin begins with a reply target, settled at once when asked, not after the channel ended, not by the client); `test/actors/channel_startup_sweep_test.dart` (1: re-armed at startup, settling a served channel inside its margin and one left closing, not a later one, a closed one or a client's). Eleven mutations, all red: no lifetime check on acceptance (1), block heights accepted (1), no lifetime check on request (1), no margin (2), no margin on acknowledgement (1), an invented timing when none is configured (2), not armed on open (1), not disarmed when the channel ends (1), settled at once (3), closing channels skipped at startup (1), clients settled at startup (1, after the test's client channel was moved inside the margin; it first survived because that channel locked years ahead) |

The command is then sent for real and checked again, since the state may have moved (libspiffy-1a5k). | V-146 | High | A refund claim was journaled when ARC answered it as a double spend | `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart` | Found by the localnet run (22 Sep). | V-147 | High | A refund was claimed on the clock; the network holds its lock time to the chain's median time past | `payment_channel_aggregate.dart`, `channel_commands.dart`, `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart`, `block_header_chain.dart`, `libspiffy_actor_system.dart`, `README.md` | Found by the localnet run (22 Sep). | V-150 | High | A channel step failed on an in-flight ARC answer that was no verdict, and a funding the network went on to hold failed the open | `payment_channel_manager_actor.dart`, `deferred_payment.dart`, `arc_actor.dart`, `wallet_messages.dart` | Found by the localnet channel run after ARC's block tracker was reset (22 Sep); two of six scenarios failed with it.
